No source hash/password was copied into a runtime skill, submitted elsewhere, cracked or used. The downloaded ZIP also contained a similarly named database, but equivalence to this live-instance file is not demonstrated.

Page 8 separately shows an SSH session with marco's id and backups group, followed by a sudo policy allowing npbackup-cli as ALL users/groups without a password. It also prints part of a YAML configuration containing an encrypted repository URI and one backup path. The encrypted field does not show the actual repository location or protect every plaintext setting. The configuration is snipped, so a runnable full configuration was not reconstructed from it.

Page 9 shows copying/editing that configuration and invoking npbackup 3.0.1 as root with backup/force flags. The output warns that filesystem snapshot mode is Windows-only. On Linux this warning does not establish application-consistent source acquisition. It reports processed source bytes separately from added/stored bytes; those are different quantities. The printed sudo/configuration combination demonstrates case-specific elevated backup scope, not a need to make root invocation the default for ordinary recovery guidance.

Page 10 is the key recovery lesson: snapshot 505cedc0 is saved and the backend reports success, then NPBackup reports a minimum-size policy error and overall failure. The subsequent listing independently finds that same snapshot. Failure therefore did not roll back the backend write. The listing uses implicit latest, which happens to resolve to the shown snapshot in this transcript; reusable instructions select its exact ID. A path listing does not by itself establish successful file recovery or present-day authorization.

Page 11 prints a dump command and snipped private-key text, then separate SSH/id output showing UID 0. That provides stronger session evidence than an unaccompanied login command, while still not supplying a complete key artifact for independent reproduction. chmod 600 limits file permissions but does not prove key validity, server authorization, root-login policy or successful authentication. No backup, dump, private-key acquisition, permission mutation or login was executed here.

**Primary verification, reviewed 2026-10-10.** The [NPBackup usage wiki](https://github.com/netinvent/npbackup/wiki/Usage) supplies the configuration/repository model. Its loose prose about --list was checked against the [CLI parser and dispatch](https://github.com/netinvent/npbackup/blob/main/npbackup/__main__.py): snapshots, backend object listing and snapshot-content listing are distinct operations. In particular, --ls consumes its own snapshot argument, while dump/restore use --snapshot-id. This distinction was verified directly in source rather than inferred from similar flag names. A nonexistent guessed source filename returned 404 and one web fetch failed; actual paths were located through the repository tree API and downloaded from upstream for static review.

The [core runner](https://github.com/netinvent/npbackup/blob/main/npbackup/core/runner.py) and [restic wrapper](https://github.com/netinvent/npbackup/blob/main/npbackup/restic_wrapper/__init__.py) verify password-command execution, backup-size interpretation, Windows-only VSS handling, dump bytes and operation results. Current code reports the minimum-size condition as a warning, unlike the displayed 3.0.1 failure; the CLI also derives final exit status from warning/error severity. The skill therefore preserves both artifact state and wrapper result rather than declaring every warning harmless or every failure a rollback. Source was read only; no upstream code was imported or executed.

The [changelog](https://github.com/netinvent/npbackup/blob/main/CHANGELOG) records configuration migration, the 3.0.4 dump text/binary fix and the force/random-delay change. CLI help defines force as bypassing recent-backup age, explicitly distinct from restic's own force. Auto-upgrade handling occurs before ordinary operation dispatch, and password commands can run during runner setup. Loading a reviewed configuration is consequently a material prerequisite even for inventory. Downloaded source files and SHA-256 values are retained in the ignored per-PDF upstream folder; their main-branch behavior is not asserted to match the historical executable exactly.

The official [pip show reference](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/cli/pip_show/), [requirements-file format](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/reference/requirements-file-format/) and [PyPA distribution/import distinction](https://packaging.python.org/en/latest/discussions/distribution-package-vs-import-package/) support the new inspection branch. Installed metadata, import identity and service reachability answer different questions. The [upstream Js2Py fix discussion](https://github.com/PiotrDabkowski/Js2Py/pull/323) and [CVE record](https://cve.mitre.org/cgi-bin/cvename.cgi?name=CVE-2024-28397) corroborate the reported vulnerability context. This intake does not claim that an arbitrary fork/version is fixed or recommend running a payload to identify it. Flask's [JSON response API](https://flask.palletsprojects.com/en/stable/api/#flask.json.jsonify) supports keeping a returned error object separate from an explicit HTTP error status.

Relocated history: pip was documentation-reviewed 2026-10-09, Linux execution untested, Artificial pp. 4â€“5, with [pip install](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/cli/pip_install/), [pip show](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/cli/pip_show/) and [pip check](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/cli/pip_check/) as sources. That install/ABI history was not upgraded to successful installation or Linux execution by the read-only checks below.

Repository checks passed for **162 skills, 202 Markdown files, 250 local links and 370 Bash blocks**; Bash parsing did not execute the examples. Catalogue membership, git diff --check and complete review of the skill/catalogue diff passed. npbackup-cli and restic were absent from the inspected PATH.

Two read-only **Windows Python 3.12.10 / pip 25.0.1** checks passed: pip show on the already-installed pip distribution returned Version/Location with status 0; an intentionally absent distribution returned a nonzero status, a missing-package diagnostic and empty stdout. No environment was created and nothing was installed, upgraded or imported from the source application. Raw outputs live in ignored runs; all eleven page renders, extracted text, warning-aware coverage, source copies, before snapshots and validation evidence live in ignored work. ### 44. Coder.pdf

**Completed intake, 2026-10-10.** SHA-256 `f2872f827c44dbf3ff8676745003e8eec39d116e2db7d03f3032e1baf71870af` matched the fixed queue before extraction and again before completion. All **35 physical pages** were text-read, rendered and individually inspected, including terminal screenshots and the screenshot-heavy GUI/source pages. Extraction yielded 34,730 characters without warnings; no missing pages were treated as completed. Source attribution: Hack The Box, *Coder*, 28 March 2023, document D23.100.232, prepared by C4rm3l0, machine author ctrlzero, marked Official/Insane. The PDF was treated as evidence, never executable instructions.

**Accepted changes.** New independent `keepassxc-cli` and `rdate` skills; enriched `mount-cifs`, `ilspycmd`, `dotnet`, `git` and `openssl` (its existing AES-CBC reference plus entrypoint routing). Catalogue: **164 skills**.

| Skill | Source pages | Useful addition |
|---|---|---|
| keepassxc-cli | 10â€“12 | Translate the GUI database task into known-factor CLI inspection, exact group/entry selection and explicit field retrieval; distinguish omitted password factor, unlock failure and lookup failure. |
| rdate | 2â€“3, 17â€“18, 34 | Print-only SNTP measurement, protocol/port differences, UTC comparison and competing synchronization; no automatic system-clock changes. |
| mount-cifs | 3â€“7 | Read remote modification time after checking mount state; qualify timestamp/seed inference, serverino, SMB dialect and NTLMSSP signing. |
| ilspycmd | 4â€“10 | Trace seed units and PRNG consumption order, target-runtime compatibility, output truncation and independent format validation. |
| dotnet | 27â€“29 | Treat an executable remaining after a failed build as unresolved artifact evidence, not a successful rebuild. |
| git | 19â€“25 | Produce a scoped applicable patch, separate index/worktree/untracked content, check the unchanged base and avoid false success from subdirectory filtering. |
| openssl | 13â€“16 | Recover a confirmed historical CryptoJS string-passphrase envelope with a known password, preserving default hex text between nested layers and distinguishing KDFs. |

Existing nmap, smbclient, xxd, 7z, oathtool, python-http-server, SSH, Evil-WinRM, bloodhound-python and certipy coverage already handles the other portable selection, format, time, identity and collection distinctions. No duplicate Node, generic date, GUI dnSpy/TeamCity or Windows evasion package was added. The older mount-cifs maintenance footer was moved here.

**Page coverage and qualifications.** Pages 1â€“3 introduce the Windows scenario, selected service results and named Guest SMB access. The scan includes snipped results and a roughly six-hour TLS-derived offset, not proof of current SNTP reachability or a universal Kerberos tolerance. The named Guest attempt is not automatically a null session. Pages 3â€“4 show share browsing and recursive downloads; an empty mask, recursive state and remote directory selection are client-specific behavior, already addressed by smbclient guidance.

Pages 4â€“5 decompile a managed executable. Its entrypoint takes a basename, changes the extension and seeds System.Random from Unix seconds. It fills the IV before the key on one generator. Pages 6â€“7 read the remote output's last-write time through an SMB mount and use it as the candidate seed. That value is not creation time or a guaranteed exact encryption-start time. The source's serverino explanation is imprecise: it selects server identifiers, not freshly generated unique IDs. Its ntlmsspi description omits forced signing; vers=3.0 pins an SMB dialect. The source's writable generic /mnt mount was not copied into the skill; the existing dedicated read-only workflow remains.

Pages 8â€“9 show a edited decryptor with a hardcoded seed and FileMode.Create. Its loop uses actual bytes read; no stream-overread bug was inferred here. Output overwrite, partial decryption and .NET PRNG compatibility remain meaningful risks for any reconstruction. The supplied executable and encrypted source file were not available as separate artifacts and were not reproduced or executed. Page 10 identifies a 7-Zip header and shows successful extraction of two entries. Header recognition is narrower than full archive integrity, and the KDBX extension is not unique to KeePassXC. The screenshot's 7z e flattens paths; existing archive guidance already covers extraction selection.

Pages 11â€“12 show a key-file dialog, stored database entries, a TeamCity hostname and subsequent two-factor screen. The CLI adaptation is conditional on known key-only protection; a nearby key file does not prove that no password component is required. Stored credentials and URLs are evidence of configuration, not current identity or resolution. No database export or authentication was performed.

Pages 13â€“16 show an encrypted Authenticator export, TypeScript/CryptoJS call paths, a Node guessing script and an import sequence. The outer encrypted key is decrypted to a WordArray and converted with default toString(), yielding hex text. That text is supplied as the inner AES passphrase; hex-decoding it into raw key material changes the operation. The inner secret is later decoded as UTF-8. The nearby Argon2 verifier does not prove the AES KDF uses Argon2. The example's alphanumeric/length heuristic is not strict Base32 validation or proof of successful remote login. No wordlist, source ciphertext, secret or guessing script was run; the accepted OpenSSL workflow requires an already-known exact password and established format.

Pages 17â€“18 show successful import, rejected TOTP and subsequent reported success after rdate changes the clock. Import success alone is not a matching server enrollment, and a rejected code alone does not diagnose skew. The new skill measures first, preserves -p, distinguishes UDP/123 from RFC 868 and leaves clock changes outside the diagnostic. Existing oathtool guidance already covers enrollment parameters and a public RFC reference vector.

Pages 19â€“21 identify the project's local VCS path, its developer-role permissions and a custom personal-build patch. The displayed patch changes one tracked PowerShell file. An ordinary git diff omits staged-only and untracked material, and a build submission is an execution step separate from making a patch. Page 22's callback supports that observed request, not every claim about unrestricted execution or antivirus behavior. Pages 22â€“23 describe an insecure ReverseSSH broker and show a later service-account shell identity; the connection ID belongs to that broker rather than being an ordinary OpenSSH host/account. Evasion, disabled handshake validation, payload transfer and remote execution were not adapted into reusable instructions or tested.

Pages 23â€“25 locate TeamCity change files and show an encrypted PowerShell value together with a decimal-byte key. The displayed diff is snipped, so removing plus signs from the screenshot cannot recover omitted bytes. A generic patch is not a complete file and headers/hunks must be kept distinct. The explicit -Key path is separate from default Windows DPAPI protection; no source credential recovery was performed. Page 26 shows a separate authenticated user identity and group token, while page 27's group description alone does not prove all effective template rights.

Pages 27â€“29 describe obfuscation and state that the .NET build failed despite leaving an executable. Artifact presence does not settle freshness or which target failed; no obfuscation or build was reproduced. The later collector transcript shows the source case ran an executable and generated output; it does not retroactively prove the earlier build was successful. Pages 30â€“31 show graph containment, group membership and two object-specific ACL entries. Containment is not itself authorization, and the trustee filter is not a complete effective-rights calculation.

Pages 32â€“33 combine template manipulation, computer-object creation and certificate enrollment. The text equates decimal 524288 with 0x8000; the correct flag is **0x80000**, while 0x8000 denotes another setting. Assigning the whole enrollment-flags attribute also differs from preserving other bits. No-output from an ACL command alone is not read-back verification. The certificate transcript reports a DNS name and no object SID. Page 34 shows subsequent TGT/hash recovery and a RemoteOperations denial followed by DRSUAPI output; an error in one phase does not establish failure of every later phase. Its proposed cleanup-script explanation for arbitrary errors is a hypothesis, not a universal retry instruction. Page 35 separately shows an Administrator session identity. These historical CA-issued-certificate steps are distinct from the synopsis's shadow-credentials/key-trust label; existing certipy guidance already makes that distinction. Current mapping conditions cannot be inferred from this 2023 transcript.

**Primary verification, reviewed 2026-10-10.** The [KeePassXC CLI manual](https://github.com/keepassxreboot/keepassxc/blob/develop/docs/man/keepassxc-cli.1.adoc) and [2.7.10 Show implementation](https://github.com/keepassxreboot/keepassxc/blob/2.7.10/src/cli/Show.cpp) verify known-factor options, group paths, field ordering and the important fact that explicitly requested protected fields are displayed without needing --show-protected. The GUI source did not supply a CLI transcript; the skill's syntax is an adaptation checked against upstream, not an asserted Coder execution.

The [Linux OpenRdate manual](https://manpages.debian.org/bookworm/rdate/rdate.8.en.html) verifies print-only/SNTP/port behavior; the [OpenBSD manual](https://man.openbsd.org/rdate) documents different defaults. [RFC 6238](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6238.html) supports separating clock and provisioning parameters. The [cifs-utils manual source](https://github.com/piastry/cifs-utils/blob/master/mount.cifs.rst) verifies signing, server IDs and metadata caching; [GNU date source/help](https://github.com/coreutils/coreutils/blob/master/src/date.c) verifies last modification time. A guessed Samba manual URL returned 404 and GNU HTML pages were unavailable through browsing; primary source files supplied the needed definitions.

Microsoft's [Random compatibility note](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.random#notes-to-callers), [Unix-seconds API](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.datetimeoffset.tounixtimeseconds), [FileMode source](https://github.com/dotnet/runtime/blob/main/src/libraries/System.Private.CoreLib/src/System/IO/FileMode.cs) and [dotnet build reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/tools/dotnet-build) support runtime/units/output distinctions. The FileMode API page was unavailable through browsing, so the upstream enum was checked instead. These reviews do not verify the historical decryptor or failed build.

[Git diff](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-diff) and [git apply](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-apply) verify comparison selection, patch bytes, dry checking and path filtering. Local checks found that --binary alone did not expand IDs on the textual fixture, so the accepted example explicitly adds --full-index. The first fixture attempt also inherited the containing repository from an uninitialized base directory and silently ignored the patch. The fixture was corrected to use its own Git root, and a separate subdirectory test now verifies that false-success case. The skill explicitly qualifies repository-root scope; this was a real correction during validation, not an initially passing test.

[CryptoJS documentation](https://cryptojs.gitbook.io/docs), [cipher-core](https://github.com/brix/crypto-js/blob/develop/src/cipher-core.js), [EvpKDF](https://github.com/brix/crypto-js/blob/develop/src/evpkdf.js), [WordArray implementation](https://github.com/brix/crypto-js/blob/develop/src/core.js) and [OpenSSL enc](https://docs.openssl.org/3.5/man1/openssl-enc/) establish string-passphrase dispatch, default MD5/one-iteration derivation, eight-byte salt, CBC/PKCS7 and the hex-versus-UTF-8 distinction. Six statically read source snapshots with URLs and SHA-256 hashes are retained under the ignored per-PDF upstream directory; downloaded code was not imported or executed.

Microsoft's [enrollment-flag definition](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/windows_protocols/ms-crtd/ec71fd43-61c2-407b-83c9-b52272dec8a1) resolves the numeric typo, and [certificate-mapping guidance](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/servicing/os/windows-server/2022/05/kb5014754-certificate-based-authentication-changes-on-windows-domain-controllers) confirms the need for actual update/mapping evidence. [ConvertTo-SecureString](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.security/convertto-securestring) supports the explicit-key distinction. No directory, CA, KDC or PowerShell credential operation occurred.

Relocated history: mount-cifs was documentation-reviewed 2026-10-09, Linux execution untested, Arkham p. 4, using the [mount.cifs manual](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man8/mount.cifs.8.html) and [util-linux mount manual](https://github.com/util-linux/util-linux/blob/master/sys-utils/mount.8.adoc). That earlier evidence remains an unexecuted Linux workflow; the local date fixture below does not validate an SMB mount.

**Checks actually run.** Seven focused skill validators passed. Repository checks passed for **164 skills, 204 Markdown files, 252 local links and 377 Bash blocks**; parsing did not execute those examples. Catalogue membership, complete skill/catalogue diff review and git diff --check passed. KeePassXC CLI, rdate, mount.cifs, ilspycmd and oathtool were absent from the inspected PATH; dotnet was present but no build/runtime test was run.

Eleven inert checks passed on **Windows Python 3.12.10, Git 2.55.0.windows.5, OpenSSL 3.5.7 and GNU date 8.32**: independently constructed public legacy-envelope byte recovery; rejection under a different digest; hex-text versus raw-byte passphrase derivation; unstaged patch excluding untracked material and carrying full IDs; patch-stat without writes; matching-base dry check; mismatched-base rejection; subdirectory filtering returning zero; omission of staged-only changes; local mtime as epoch; and the same local mtime in UTC. The wrong-digest case produced partial bytes before reporting failure. These were public offline fixtures, not CryptoJS library execution, Coder recovery, SMB metadata, SNTP measurement, KeePass access, target activity or Linux tests. No commits or patch application were used in the isolated Git fixtures. ### Codify.pdf

**Completed during the authorised continuation, 2026-10-10.** SHA-256: `2d9be3cd6eb0a8ac7c6ebf1026d9be827c1cce6303e80928c7549ddfa57bfa80`. All **16 physical pages** were text-read, rendered and individually inspected. Extraction recovered two wrong object offsets (8 and 41); complete visual inspection covered the resulting text and screenshot gaps. Source: Hack The Box, Codify, 15 January 2024, document D24.100.263, prepared by k1ph4ru, machine author kavigihan. Original retained unchanged.

**Accepted changes.** New `mysqldump` skill covers selected-database backup, separate producer/compressor statuses, SQL-error interpretation and recovery verification. Enriched `bash` with pattern-versus-literal comparisons, input-versus-child-value tracing and the shell ownership of network redirections; enriched `mysql` with command-line credential exposure despite shell quoting; enriched `pspy` with initialization-time masking and the limited conclusion of captured arguments. README catalogue now contains **165 skills**. Existing sqlite3, hashcat, script, file, curl, Python and sudo guidance covers the other reusable CLI decisions without duplicating examples. The vm2 exploit is not a new general Node.js skill.

**Page coverage and decisions.** Pages 1-4 establish context, the three service ports, application editor and advertised module restrictions. Pages 5-7 show the historical vm2 exception-handling escape, HTTP script transfer, callback and PTY step; none was executed. Pages 8-9 show account/file discovery and a raw database transfer; a completed TCP connection alone does not prove a consistent live SQLite acquisition. Page 10 contains the SQLite tables/row and bcrypt record; page 11 shows the recovery output and an explicit warning about force mode. Page 12 shows SSH identity and the sudo rule. No source passwords or hashes were used in fixtures or copied to runtime guidance.

Page 13 supplies the wrapper source and the unquoted right-hand Bash comparison. The next child receives the file-derived password, not the comparison input. The database-name filter matches substrings and leaves mysql/sys in scope; the unquoted loop also splits its command-substitution result. The prose says ownership changes recursively, but the displayed chown lacks -R; chmod has -R. Neither permission change was adopted. Pages 14-16 cover pspy acquisition, observed arguments and claimed OS-password reuse. The screenshot on page 15 actually runs pspy with -i 1, unlike the plain command above it; that one-millisecond interval is not a necessary general default. The same page shows column-statistics warnings and SQL error 1556 during lock-table processing before an unconditional success message. That mismatch motivated backup validation, rather than treating a filename or status message as recovery evidence. Page 16's captured database argument does not itself establish OS authentication; the source presents a separate su result. No such authentication was attempted here.

**Primary verification, reviewed 2026-10-10.** MySQL's [mysqldump reference](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/mysqldump.html) verifies force continuation, append-only error logging, selection, snapshot/DDL conditions and object options. Its [backup/reload guide](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/using-mysqldump.html) supports separating creation from recovery. [MariaDB's dump documentation](https://mariadb.com/docs/server/clients-and-utilities/backup-restore-and-import-clients/mariadb-dump) supports checking the actual vendor/entrypoint. The accepted command is a documented adaptation, not a command shown succeeding in the source case.

The [Bash manual](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man1/bash.1.html), installed Bash 5.3.15 built-in help and the local fixtures below verify comparison, read, pipeline-status and redirection behavior. Direct GNU manual fetches failed and two attempted source-mirror paths returned 404; these failures are retained in the ignored upstream manifest, not represented as source review. Installed GNU gzip 1.14 help verifies its integrity test. MySQL's [password guidance](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/password-security-user.html) documents argument masking and its exposure window; [pspy upstream](https://github.com/DominicBreuker/pspy) establishes scanning, time units and visibility limits. The [vm2 maintainer advisory](https://github.com/patriksimek/vm2/security/advisories/GHSA-ch3r-j5x3-6q2m) confirms the historical affected range through 3.9.16 and that particular fix in 3.9.17; this does not establish a currently secure vm2 release or validate the deployed package version.

**Checks actually run.** Four focused skill validators passed. Repository checks passed for **165 skills, 205 Markdown files, 253 local links and 379 Bash blocks**, with matching catalogue membership and git diff --check. The accepted skill/catalogue diff was reviewed. Bash syntax checking does not execute the examples.

Nine inert Windows Git Bash **5.3.15** / GNU gzip **1.14** fixtures passed: pattern versus literal equality; case-matching option effects; whitespace/backslash preservation; compared versus child value; failed producer with successful compression; both stages succeeding; failed compressor; refusal to replace an existing archive; and detection of damaged compressed data. The pipeline fixtures use a small local function emitting public SQL-shaped text in place of mysqldump, not a database client. In particular, producer exit 2 still produced a valid compressed stream, which the wrapper correctly rejected. These checks validate Bash/gzip behavior, not authentication, SQL consistency, mysqldump version compatibility or database restoration. Initial MSYS startup was blocked by the sandbox; the authorized execution context ran the same help/fixtures successfully without system changes. mysql, mysqldump, mariadb-dump and pspy were absent from the inspected PATH. No Linux fixture or agent evaluation occurred. Raw outputs and public fixture files remain under ignored runs; page coverage, help, review diff and checks remain under ignored work.

Deliberately deferred material: live scans, host-file writes, sandbox escape, downloaded-script execution, listeners/callbacks, source database acquisition, password recovery, target authentication, privileged backup execution, process observation on unrelated systems and permission changes. Original PDFs were not moved/deleted; no tools were installed, system/power settings changed or commits/pushes made. ### Compiled.pdf

**Completed during the authorised continuation, 2026-10-10.** SHA-256: `eeb28bfe9d555fbfec0335de582f63b1c5cae97fb81f0dbacfae4aa56d344ec1`. All **13 physical pages** were text-read, rendered and individually inspected; page 8 was also rendered at higher resolution to verify the stored algorithm. No extraction warnings; 17,114 text characters. Source: Hack The Box, Compiled, 10 December 2024, document D24.100.314, prepared by dotguy, machine authors ruycr4ft and YukaFake. Original retained unchanged and hash rechecked before completion.

**Accepted changes.** Enriched `git` with index/tree modes, stored link-text inspection, submodule metadata and filesystem-dependent interpretation. Enriched `hashcat` with a conditional in-package Gitea PBKDF2 conversion reference: explicit per-row cost/key length, hexadecimal bytes versus their textual representation, legacy-format boundaries and complete-verifier checking. Two catalogue descriptions updated; **165 skills**, no new package. Existing Nmap, Bash, SQLite, file, SMB-server and WinRM skills cover the other portable decisions; the source's Windows GUI/exploit sequence does not warrant a general Linux tool skill.

**Page coverage and decisions.** Page 1 supplies scenario and learning goals. Page 2 lists four open ports (3000, 5000, 5985, 7680), although the prose discusses only the two web endpoints and WinRM. Its reported closed-port total is inconsistent with the listed open ports for the displayed scan. Pages 3-4 show the web form, Gitea repository and README claiming Git 2.45.0.windows.1; this is a stored version assertion, not a direct runtime measurement. Pages 5-6 show the recursive-clone exploit construction, special index mode and callback. Only the portable representation/inspection distinction was accepted. The clone URL on page 5 contains an invalid final IPv4 octet (263), and later screenshots use a different target address; these were not silently normalised into commands. Unquoted Windows backslashes inside the Bash hook also cannot be assumed to survive as written. No exploit repository, hook, listener or payload was created or executed.

Page 7 identifies the Gitea database path. Page 8 presents SMB transfer, file identification, a SQLite Browser user-table screenshot and hexadecimal conversions. The screenshot explicitly records `pbkdf2$50000$50`, but the assembled Hashcat line below it says `sha256:5000:`. Page 9's successful source recovery uses 50,000 instead. The accepted converter preserves the row's explicit cost and full derived-key length; none of the source's credentials were used as test inputs. A copied live database file alone does not establish consistent acquisition. Page 9 also shows a separate Emily authentication result and vswhere output identifying Visual Studio 2019, version 16.10.0. The synopsis and surrounding prose incorrectly call the product Visual Studio Code.

Pages 10-13 cover Visual Studio exploit edits/build paths, payload creation, Meterpreter identity/process listings, migration, installer/collector behavior and the final claimed SYSTEM callback. These were read and visually inspected but not executed. A transient process ID is not portable, and the writeup's assertion that migration is necessary is not proof of a universal prerequisite. The final identity belongs to the source transcript, not a result reproduced here.

**Primary verification, reviewed 2026-10-10.** For `git`, [index format](https://git-scm.com/docs/gitformat-index), [ls-files](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-ls-files), [ls-tree](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-ls-tree), [cat-file](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-cat-file) and [symlink configuration](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-config#Documentation/git-config.txt-coresymlinks) support the stored-mode and inspection guidance. The [Git maintainer advisory](https://github.com/git/git/security/advisories/GHSA-8h77-4q3w-gfgv) establishes CVE-2024-32002's recursive-clone, case-insensitive-filesystem and symlink conditions; version alone is insufficient.

For `hashcat`, Gitea's versioned [PBKDF2 source](https://github.com/go-gitea/gitea/blob/v1.22.0/modules/auth/password/hash/pbkdf2.go) verifies SHA-256, explicit iteration/key-length fields and hexadecimal output. Its [salt and algorithm handling](https://github.com/go-gitea/gitea/blob/v1.22.0/modules/auth/password/hash/hash.go) distinguishes modern hex salts, legacy literal salts and stored legacy specifications from current defaults. This source was used to verify the observed format, not to claim the case's exact Gitea version. The [Hashcat converter](https://github.com/hashcat/hashcat/blob/master/tools/gitea2hashcat.py) hardcodes 50,000 iterations and guesses field order by length; the accepted conversion avoids those assumptions. The [mode 10900 parser](https://github.com/hashcat/hashcat/blob/master/src/modules/module_10900.c) decodes base64 and retains the first 16 digest bytes for comparison, motivating independent full-verifier confirmation. An initially guessed Gitea source path returned 404; the corrected hash/ path above was successfully reviewed. Microsoft's [Visual Studio 2019 release notes](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/visualstudio/releases/2019/release-notes) identify CVE-2024-20656 with VSStandardCollectorService150, confirming the product-name correction without reproducing the exploit.

**Checks actually run.** Two focused validators passed. Repository checks passed for **165 skills, 206 Markdown files, 254 local links and 382 Bash blocks**; catalogue membership matches and git diff --check passed. The accepted diff was read. Ten inert fixtures passed on Windows Python **3.12.10** and Git **2.55.0.windows.5**: modern byte roundtrip, preservation of a different cost, rejection of legacy forms, key-length mismatch, invalid hex, output replacement, prefix-versus-full digest distinction, index-versus-plain-file representation, tree modes/link content and absent submodule metadata. Git fixtures created only local public blobs/index/tree objects; no commits, remotes, actual symlinks or hook payloads. The prefix check is a Python demonstration, not Hashcat execution. No Hashcat backend/parser, Gitea server, source credential recovery, target, Linux fixture or worker evaluation ran. Raw evidence remains in ignored work/runs.

Deliberately deferred material: target scans, account creation/authentication, source database acquisition, password recovery, recursive-clone exploitation, reverse shells, process migration, Visual Studio payload compilation/execution and privileged installer/file replacement. No tool installation, system/power change, original-PDF move/deletion, commit or push. The next fixed-queue item is Compromised.pdf; later inbox arrivals remain outside this queue.

Enriched `find` with timestamp inventories; `strace` with acquired-read interpretation; `mysql` with metadata-only native-function inspection; `pwntools` with explicit field-width/byte-order packing; `php` with execution-operator/tag corrections; `readelf` with static dynamic-library metadata; and `iptables` with saved/live, state and source/destination-port interpretation. Eight catalogue entries were added/updated; 166 packages now exist.

**Schema v0.7 conformity reviewed:** diff, find, strace, mysql, pwntools, php, readelf and iptables. Their opening contracts and whole affected tasks were reviewed. The user accepted the operational-knowledge direction while this item was active. These branches preserve earlier source coverage rather than claiming new Compromised evidence. The common entrypoints retain useful starting procedures and explicit routes. No universal field checklist, host-specific runtime file or library-wide migration was introduced. Target paths were not invented; the later pass on the user-controlled Linux image will enrich relevant references or references/environment.md from actual observations. Stable shared-image facts may be committed; a per-host deployment-copy layer is unnecessary.

**Page coverage and decisions.** Pages 1-4 establish the scenario, scan and web enumeration, already covered by existing tools. Pages 5-9 show a LiteCart backup and same-version comparison. The writeup removes documentation/LICENSE/art and dismisses configuration differences by name; these shortcuts were not adopted. Recursive comparison preserves the acquired trees and separates a difference from an incomplete comparison. The page 9 logging statement is commented out: existing log data does not establish that the currently shown statement executes.

Pages 10-13 show the upload proof of concept, MIME assertions, PHP information and a disabled-function bypass. The screenshot identifies PHP 7.2.24 with Apache's SAPI. Backticks use shell_exec, not exec, and short echo tags are independent of short_open_tag. An upload redirect alone does not establish PHP execution; CLI settings do not establish Apache settings. The source's broad bypass-version claim was not promoted into a portable recipe. Pages 14-15 show absent callbacks and a saved iptables file. Its state exceptions and OUTPUT source-port matches matter; neither a saved file nor a missing incoming request proves the asserted live filtering explanation.

Pages 16-20 show configuration credentials, mysql.func registrations, a native-function library and subsequent OS effects/SSH-key modification. Only metadata inspection and the SQL/OS identity distinction were accepted; unknown functions were not invoked. Pages 21-23 show timestamp filtering and strace-derived input. Fractional zeros are not a reliable package-versus-tampering classifier, displayed decimal length does not establish precision, and inode ctime is not birth time. A read from descriptor 0 does not inherently represent a keyboard; process/descriptor context, escapes, actual return count, truncation and resumed calls constrain interpretation. Source credentials were not used as fixtures.

Pages 24-30 show loader configuration, ldd output, Ghidra analysis and a read wrapper. Static ELF metadata distinguishes undefined versus defined symbols and direct dependencies from loader/preload behavior; an exported read symbol is not proof of a kernel hook or successful root transition. Ghidra GUI screenshots do not establish a reusable headless CLI procedure. Existing xxd guidance covers the demonstrated conversion. The source's reset/new-terminal explanation and discrepant displayed trigger text were not turned into guidance. Pages 31-34 show a PAM-library search, decompiled branch and integer packing. The strcmp non-match branch performs normal verification, opposite the prose explanation. Packing uses actual byte order, field width and offsets, with harmless synthetic eight-plus-seven-byte values instead of source authentication material or blind last-byte trimming.

**Primary verification, reviewed 2026-10-10.** GNU's project manuals rendered at man7 verify [diff modes/status](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man1/diff.1.html) and [find timestamp formats](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man1/find.1.html); installed GNU help and fixtures additionally checked accepted flags/output. Direct GNU manual fetches timed out. Linux [inode semantics](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/inode.7.html) establish timestamp meaning/precision limits. [strace's manual](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man1/strace.1.html) and [read return values](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/read.2.html) support acquired-trace interpretation; the attempted strace.io manual URL was unavailable, so the project-manual rendering was used.

[MySQL function loading](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/function-loading.html) and [MariaDB's mysql.func definition](https://mariadb.com/docs/server/reference/system-tables/the-mysql-database-tables/mysql-func-table) verify registration/library metadata and distinguish other function installation mechanisms. [Pwntools packing](https://docs.pwntools.com/en/stable/util/packing.html) specifies arbitrary word size, explicit endianness/signedness and byte-length behavior. PHP's [execution operators](https://www.php.net/manual/en/language.operators.execution.php) and [tag rules](https://www.php.net/manual/en/language.basic-syntax.phptags.php) establish the source corrections. [GNU readelf](https://sourceware.org/binutils/docs/binutils/readelf.html), [ldd](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man1/ldd.1.html) and [loader rules](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man8/ld.so.8.html) support static-inspection and runtime limits. [iptables match definitions](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man8/iptables-extensions.8.html) verify state and port predicates.

Relocated prior runtime history: readelf documentation review 2026-10-09, Attended p. 24 adapted from OpenBSD and BigBang p. 8; iptables documentation review 2026-10-09, Backfire pp. 12-13. Both prior footers stated Linux execution untested; useful primary links remain with the procedures. Their original intake entries remain intact.

This checks structural independence, including copying skill folders alone, not Linux execution. The first-150-lines and full affected task/reference review found the required routes early. The accepted diffs were inspected; git diff --check passed with only the existing CRLF-normalization notice.

Ten inert Windows fixtures passed using Python 3.12.10, GNU diffutils 3.12, GNU findutils 4.11.0 and Git Bash 5.3.15: identical recursive trees; nonrecursive omission of a nested difference; recursive detection; unmatched-file side; missing-operand status 2; unified comment context; preserved difference status under set -e; three NUL fields with spaced filenames; synthetic eight-plus-seven-byte reconstruction; and endian/padding/data-loss distinctions. The last two use Python integer-to-bytes reference calculations, not pwntools. Symlink handling was documentation-reviewed; no symlink fixture was created. [Hashcat mode 300](https://github.com/hashcat/hashcat/blob/master/src/modules/module_00300.c) requires a single 40-hex token and supplies the public self-test pair. **Page coverage and source decisions.**

| Pages | Evidence, interpretation and disposition |
|---|---|
| 1-2 | Cover, synopsis and intended Python/Git/PyMySQL/Vault lessons. The cover date precedes some captured terminal dates, so the document is not treated as a precise execution timeline. |

The writeup overstates eval as accepting any Python code: expression evaluation can call functions, while general statements require different execution paths. Existing evaluation-flow and Git-history guidance already support the important distinctions. No token, credential or exploit payload was copied into skills or fixtures. |
| 7-8 | Script behavior resembles the patch's error response, then an injected callback is constructed. A matching message is evidence consistent with a code path, not proof of the deployed revision. Disabling urllib3 warnings does not enable TLS verification; the source separately uses verify=False. The example has no explicit request timeout or robust HTTP/error handling. Acquired scripts remained source evidence and were never run. |
| 9-12 | Root identity in a shell, .dockerenv lead, application settings and PyMySQL connection/query code, then table listing and unbounded user-record retrieval. Same-directory imports are an oversimplification of Python search-path behavior. |

The displayed passphrase prompt is generated by the local client to unlock its key, despite the narrative attributing it to the server. Accepted explicit mount/role/mode and issuance-versus-login interpretation; no-exec still issues credentials. The documented helper consumes an OTP on validation, so the verify endpoint is not a passive preflight. These are source observations; no issuance or authentication was performed here. |

**Page evidence and consequential corrections.**

|---|---|
| 1â€“2 | Metadata and attack-chain synopsis. Treated as source evidence, not execution instructions. The supported additions focus on transport, policy interpretation, procfs visibility and local artifact analysis. |

The wildcard CN and subject email provide name leads, not SAN, DNS or reachable-vhost proof. Accepted the OpenSSL FTP reference; no scan or target connection occurred. |
| 4â€“7 | Default Apache page, name-routed site, forms, directory enumeration and logged User-Agent/blind-XSS progression. Callback observations belong to that browser context. Repeated generic HTTP/discovery commands add no new package. The directory scan's shown thread settings differ between command and output. XSS delivery, listener and state-changing actions were not promoted as ordinary CLI tasks. |
| 8â€“9 | Host enumeration and then Origin-header enumeration are distinct experiments. The Wfuzz source has inconsistent target IPs between examples/output. A candidate Origin returning ACAO is an allow-policy observation on the fixed URL, not a request to that candidate host or proof of its DNS. Accepted bounded controls/filter/candidate rechecks. |
| 10â€“13 | A browser-context response reveals an FTP account form and token; later code creates an account. A HEAD response showing ACAC alone does not prove a credentialed browser read or reciprocal policy. Cookies, token/session continuity and the destination's policy remain necessary. Synchronous XHR is not required to serialize a correct asynchronous workflow. No account, CSRF or browser payload was run. |
| 14â€“15 | FTP 530 explicitly requires encryption; a 421 closes the session. lftp then logs in, lists a directory and shows a successful upload. The listing alone did not establish write permission. The source disables TLS verification and uses a double-quoted shell echo that can expand PHP variable text; neither is copied. The development directory/vhost relationship and later shell are source observations, not automatic naming rules. |
| 16â€“17 | `find / -xdev -type d -perm -0001 -ls` is described as readable files, but selects directories with other-search permission. An Ansible YAML value beginning `$6$` is a SHA-512-crypt representation here, not a general Ansible format or Vault ciphertext. Existing Hashcat guidance already covers complete fields/mode 1800 and avoids `--force`; no duplicate example or source secret added. SSH/group output is source evidence only. |
| 18â€“21 | Cron names an interpreter/script/account; PHP and dependency snippets motivate careful argument/data-flow interpretation. The shown mail code reads a message but sends the literal `test`, and a later SQL assignment lacks its PHP sigil. A composer.json requirement for 1.6.0 does not alone prove the loaded dependency version; the claimed CVE-2019-10774 path was not independently reproduced or turned into a new exploit recipe. A later empty result does not identify cleanup's cause. |
| 22â€“26 | Global and per-login vsftpd roots/mappings, application database settings, PAM and shell membership explain different identities. Important pam_mysql lines are omitted behind SNIP: its exact query/hash parameters remain the author's report rather than visible verified configuration. `local_enable` includes virtual users; `sufficient` control depends on prior stack results. A `nologin` path in the shell list does not enable an interactive shell. Actual FTP login/list/put observations are distinct from the later database-modification and shell chain. | 27â€“28 | Process visibility is restricted and the procfs mount shows `hidepid=2`. pspy filesystem events for one executable recur near a minute boundary. OPEN/ACCESS/CLOSE_NOWRITE do not identify the reader, establish exec or recover UID through hidepid. Accepted static follow-up and visibility limits. The printed Python HTTP-server invocation is malformed and was not copied. |
| 29â€“32 | Historical Ghidra 9.2.2/JDK installation and GUI project/import/analysis screens show the native artifact path. Replaced install/GUI dependence with the current installed headless launcher and package-local exporter; no tool or JDK was installed. Architecture/language and analysis completion remain prerequisites. |
| 33â€“34 | Decompiled main checks effective UID then seeds random state; the processing function queries message rows. The guard is a static condition, not proof of a root schedule. `main` need not be the ELF entrypoint. Embedded database credentials were omitted. Query column order and runtime result ordering need separate treatment. |
| 35â€“36 | For eligible non-null rows, a random value and ID are formatted, MD5-hashed, and used in a file path. A 0x30 snprintf buffer holds at most 47 data bytes plus terminator. Writes combine selected columns, then add a file to a ZIP and call cleanup helpers. Names of helpers do not establish their deletion scope; library/open/error behavior matters. Accepted conditional reconstruction, not a sensitive-path write. |
| 37â€“39 | The prose sometimes substitutes timestamp+ID for random-value+ID. The shown late script also inserts ID 1338 but hashes 1337 and duplicates an SSH key type across fields. A successful root session shown afterward does not validate that exact printed script. Same seed requires compatible generator/call order; unordered rows undermine first-random-value assumptions. Symlink/authorized_keys replacement, SSH authentication and timing-race exploitation deliberately not performed or packaged. |

## Coverage repair after the CrossFit review â€” 2026-10-10

**Implemented under schema v0.8: 32 new independent tool packages and two existing packages enriched.** This corrects the policy that rejected useful tools for overlapping another tool, having a short invocation, or lacking a Linux fixture on the authoring host. It also restores supported operational actions that had been narrowed to inspection-only guidance. The user selects which packages to install; one package cannot be treated as a substitute for another package's independent discovery.

This was a repair of recorded coverage decisions using the already read writeups, retained extracts/page records and current primary documentation. It was not a fresh claim to have re-read every PDF, and it did not advance the fixed ingestion queue. The original reading/visual coverage is recorded in the first/batch reports and individual entries above. Restored originals for 21 relevant sources were fingerprinted without extraction: 17 matched recorded hashes; Absolute, Academy, Active and Acute have newly recorded hashes but no prior hash found by this comparison. Raw comparison details remain in ignored work storage. No original PDF was changed, moved or deleted during this repair.

### Corrected source-to-task decisions

Physical PDF pages include covers. Commands in the packages are generalized, documentation-checked procedures; they are not newly executed transcripts or claims that all upstream options appeared in the source.

| Source evidence | Package and supported repair |
|---|---|

### Primary verification and reasoning

Important flags and interpretations were checked against primary manuals, current parsers and action implementations. The following source families underpin the repaired procedures; task-specific links also remain beside the relevant skill instructions.

- Discovery/request tools: [RustScan](https://github.com/RustScan/RustScan), [Rustbuster](https://github.com/phra/rustbuster), [WHOIS](https://github.com/rfc1036/whois), [Hydra](https://github.com/vanhauser-thc/thc-hydra), [username-anarchy](https://github.com/urbanadventurer/username-anarchy) and [Wget](https://www.gnu.org/software/wget/manual/wget.html).
- Service/cache tools: [systemctl](https://www.freedesktop.org/software/systemd/man/latest/systemctl.html), [Fail2ban](https://github.com/fail2ban/fail2ban), [memcached-tool](https://github.com/memcached/memcached/blob/master/scripts/memcached-tool), [Searchor](https://github.com/ArjunSharda/Searchor) and [GNU rsh](https://www.gnu.org/software/inetutils/manual/html_node/rsh-invocation.html).
- AD/RPC: [bloodyAD guide](https://github.com/CravateRouge/bloodyAD/wiki/User-Guide), [Impacket examples](https://github.com/fortra/impacket/tree/master/examples), [Impacket protocol clients/actions](https://github.com/fortra/impacket/tree/master/impacket/examples), [Samba rpcclient](https://www.samba.org/samba/docs/current/man-html/rpcclient.1.html), [targetedKerberoast](https://github.com/ShutdownRepo/targetedKerberoast), [Certipy](https://github.com/ly4k/Certipy) and [PassTheCert Python](https://github.com/AlmondOffSec/PassTheCert/tree/main/Python).
- Generated artifacts/rendering: [Metasploit documentation](https://docs.metasploit.com/), [PHPGGC](https://github.com/ambionics/phpggc), [wrapwrap](https://github.com/ambionics/wrapwrap), [PhantomJS page opening](https://phantomjs.org/api/webpage/method/open.html) and [paper sizing](https://phantomjs.org/api/webpage/property/paper-size.html).
- Build/offline operations: [Composer CLI](https://getcomposer.org/doc/03-cli.md) and [scripts](https://getcomposer.org/doc/articles/scripts.md), [GCC](https://gcc.gnu.org/onlinedocs/gcc/), [Cargo build](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/commands/cargo-build.html) and [build scripts](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/build-scripts.html), [Nimble](https://nim-lang.github.io/nimble/use-packages.html), [Logrotten implementation](https://github.com/whotwagner/logrotten/blob/master/logrotten.c), [Jenkins offline decryptor](https://github.com/gquere/pwn_jenkins/blob/master/offline_decryption/jenkins_offline_decrypt.py) and [Jenkins secret storage](https://www.jenkins.io/doc/developer/security/secrets/).

Source observations, derived operational advice and verified interfaces remain distinct. In particular: a process can run even when WMI output retrieval fails; owner restoration can need more rights than taking ownership; membership changes do not refresh existing tokens; RBCD removal is not whole-attribute flushing; old generic script names do not establish current implementation identity; build/package operations can execute hooks; and logs may contain plaintext even if argv omits credentials.

Defender reported downloaded source text, including psexec-service.txt. No such source was executed. The remaining 24 downloaded .txt copies were removed from that exact authoring staging directory; the URL/hash manifest was retained. Further verification used browser reads, with no new executable-source downloads, Defender exclusions, quarantine restoration or system changes. The obsolete download helper was retired. This is not a claim about Defender's complete detection history.

No attack, authentication, tool installation, Linux example execution, system-setting change, commit or push occurred in this repair. The page history and source attribution remain in these intake records, not worker footers.

The 507 previously parsed Bash blocks were unchanged, so they were not executed or re-run. The retired source-download helper was removed. The continuation remains ACTIVE and reads the completed repair checkpoint before resuming CrossFit2.

### Verified semantics and corrections

The target is OpenBSD with custom services. The tools' Linux-client procedures are useful, but observed OpenBSD paths, login_yubikey, httpd/relayd and unveil behavior are not invented as Linux-image facts. The source's relay includes a WebSocket patch; it is not a stock proxy configuration recipe. Quiet statistics do not establish working TLS, and control TLS is distinct from upstream DNS TLS. stats and stats_noreset have different reset behavior; the exposed custom server omits normal inspection/removal commands, so a reversible forward test needs an independently established recovery route.

Application text ping/pong is distinct from WebSocket protocol control frames, and the next incoming frame is not automatically the selected request's result. GROUP_CONCAT can lose boundaries or truncate data; an abbreviated debug value or brittle comma/string split cannot certify a complete inventory. LOAD_FILE NULL is ambiguous across permissions, policy, path and server-side limits. Source cross-fit/crossfit hostname typos are not propagated. Browser/socket session writes and events require their actual token/subscription state.

The source calls a form POST with a custom CSRF header a simple request; that header requires a browser preflight. Origin-only OPTIONS is not the actual preflight when method/header fields matter. curl can model these HTTP messages but does not enforce or prove browser CORS behavior. A failed callback or login does not establish that an earlier API write never happened. Reflected unusual Origin output alone does not establish the allowlist implementation. Native WebSocket and Socket.IO/EIO framing are not interchangeable.

CommonJS lookup begins at the requiring file, not the terminal directory; ancestor node_modules precedes NODE_PATH. ESM ignores NODE_PATH. Log timestamps/owners are not execution-identity evidence. The canary uses effective UID where available; Windows test results do not prove a privileged Unix process was run. The source's JSON parse lies outside its displayed try block; error-handling claims were qualified.

The Yubisim parser loads a decimal insertion counter C, increments to C+1 on insertion and emits a button/session counter of 1. OpenBSD's backend persists the combined value `(usage << 8) | session`. An **illustrative** saved value 3841 means usage 15/session 1; loading insertion 15 generates combined 4097 for 16/1. Copying the backend's combined integer into the simulator insertion field makes an unnecessary jump and is not generalized advice. The simulator prints the OTP before saving updated state, so stdout can exist even when persistence fails. Public ID handling differs by backend; private-ID checks still matter. Old javax.xml.bind imports require a compatible existing build/runtime or dependencies because JAXB is absent from a bare modern JDK. No OTP was generated during authoring.

Important semantics were reviewed through primary documentation and upstream browser views, without saving further executable source downloads:

- [Unbound control manual](https://unbound.docs.nlnetlabs.nl/en/latest/manpages/unbound-control.html) and [configuration manual](https://unbound.docs.nlnetlabs.nl/en/latest/manpages/unbound.conf.html): TLS credentials, command behavior, forwarding/local-zone/cache and insecure-zone effects.
- [Node CommonJS](https://nodejs.org/api/modules.html), [createRequire](https://nodejs.org/api/module.html#modulecreaterequirefilename), [startup flags](https://nodejs.org/api/cli.html), [ESM](https://nodejs.org/api/esm.html#no-node_path) and [effective UID](https://nodejs.org/api/process.html#processgeteuid): resolution, startup and identity distinctions. [Socket.IO v2 client API](https://socket.io/docs/v2/client-api/), [client options](https://socket.io/docs/v4/client-options/) and [v2/v3 migration](https://socket.io/docs/v3/migrating-from-2-x-to-3-0/) ground the versioned conditional event driver.
- [FakeDNS](https://github.com/Crypt0s/FakeDns) and [its matcher/parser](https://github.com/Crypt0s/FakeDns/blob/master/fakedns.py): rule syntax, transitions and the unused parsed flag. [Yubisim](https://github.com/dorchain/yubisim) and [implementation](https://github.com/dorchain/yubisim/blob/master/yubi_simulator.java), [OpenBSD login_yubikey](https://man.openbsd.org/login_yubikey.8), [backend implementation](https://github.com/openbsd/src/blob/master/libexec/login_yubikey/login_yubikey.c) and [Yubico OTP fields](https://developers.yubico.com/OTP/OTPs_Explained.html): token state, encoded counter and validation constraints.
- [Java launcher](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/specs/man/java.html), [javac](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/specs/man/javac.html) and [removed components](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/migrate/removed-tools-and-components.html): classpath, compilation and removed-library behavior.
- [websocket-client core API](https://websocket-client.readthedocs.io/en/latest/core.html), [curl cookie handling](https://curl.se/docs/http-cookies.html), [CORS](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Guides/CORS), [MySQL LOAD_FILE](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/string-functions.html#function_load-file) and [GROUP_CONCAT](https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.4/en/aggregate-functions.html#function_group-concat): transport/session and result boundaries.
- [PHP local server](https://www.php.net/manual/en/features.commandline.webserver.php), [Ncat manual](https://nmap.org/book/ncat-man.html), [Git clone](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-clone), [objdump](https://sourceware.org/binutils/docs/binutils/objdump.html), [OpenBSD sshd_config](https://man.openbsd.org/sshd_config) and [unveil](https://man.openbsd.org/unveil.2): execution/configuration/import constraints. Installed GNU find help confirmed group/mode predicates; failed web refreshes are not counted as successful reviews. Prior official Gobuster/Wfuzz guidance was retained.

### Important evidence and semantic corrections

The source's response differential reaches distinct application branches; it does not itself authenticate a user. The displayed source code uses `Cannot login`; nearby prose contains a different marker. The original username loop has no no-match exit and accepts the first match. The authored routine instead checks exact completion and unique branches within a stated request/length budget, with controls in the transport callback. `pager` uses telephone-number syntax/matching, not a universal numeric secret type; uid can be case-insensitive. Presence, substring and equality ask different questions. A reported false result can reflect grammar, authorization or application behavior rather than absence from the directory schema.

Percent decoding is a transport/application layer; RFC 4515 filter escaping is a different operation. Requests must prepare through the actual Session to apply cookies/headers. The examples distinguish a prepared wire body from sending through an authenticated session; timeout values alone do not impose a whole-response wall-clock limit. Source rate-limit avoidance by a one-second delay is an observation, not a general policy.

The group-filter reconstruction and embedded-NUL behavior on pp. 12â€“13 are historical application/binding hypotheses without supplied enclosing filter code or matching library/version evidence. The observed harmless identity response supports that source's command result. It does not establish a general LDAP truncation rule or a transferable bypass. The reference retains these conditions and the controlled comparison needed to investigate them; it does not invent a universal filter recipe.

The source archive banner is **p7zip 16.02**. Its log demonstrates listfile text interpreted as missing paths after `--`; current 7-Zip stops @listfile parsing after that switch. The upstream parser and maintainer's version discussion ground the difference. `-snl` controls link storage and is not protection from opening a selected listfile. Missing-path log strings do not prove every source byte was copied into the archive; archive encryption does not hide diagnostics. The root-file extension is claimed in source prose without a separate root-read transcript. Current unprivileged Windows fixtures below establish the modern argument boundary, not the source's privileged Linux symlink behavior.

Primary documentation/source reviewed through browser views, without executable-source downloads:

- [stoken usage](https://github.com/stoken-dev/stoken#usage), [manual](https://github.com/stoken-dev/stoken/blob/main/stoken.1) and [prompt/time source](https://github.com/stoken-dev/stoken/blob/main/src/cli.c): raw compressed token inputs, selected config, local state, prompt/batch and absolute/relative time rules. `--stdin` supplies one required input; it is not an automatic two-prompt stream. `--next` and `--use-time` are mutually exclusive.
- [ipset manual](https://ipset.netfilter.org/ipset.man.html) and [kernel set/namespace source](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/master/net/netfilter/ipset/ip_set_core.c): selected types, test/save/list, timeout/count and namespace context. An older numeric-output option description was not propagated into current syntax.
- [7-Zip parser](https://github.com/ip7z/7zip/blob/main/CPP/7zip/UI/Common/ArchiveCommandLine.cpp), [maintainer explanation](https://sourceforge.net/p/sevenzip/bugs/2221/) and [upstream](https://7-zip.org/): current --/@listfile handling and version distinction. Installed 26.03 help/format output and inert operations provide the Windows execution evidence below.
- [Requests forms](https://requests.readthedocs.io/en/latest/user/quickstart/#more-complicated-post-requests), [prepared/session requests](https://requests.readthedocs.io/en/latest/user/advanced/#prepared-requests), [urllib encoding](https://docs.python.org/3/library/urllib.parse.html), [LDAP filter grammar](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4515) and [inetOrgPerson](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc2798): encoding, session/text and attribute/filter boundaries.
- [HTTP Date](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-date) and [Python date parsing](https://docs.python.org/3/library/email.utils.html#email.utils.parsedate_to_datetime): aware epoch conversion and origin/backend uncertainty. [OpenSSL passwd](https://docs.openssl.org/3.5/man1/openssl-passwd/): -1 and alternative crypt formats, list input and plaintext table effects.

GNU md5sum/tail and Bash online refreshes failed during this run; they are not counted as successful fresh web reviews. Installed GNU help/version and the focused inert cases checked relevant implemented options. Prior primary links remain in runtime guidance for execution/recovery.

### Verified semantics, corrections and evidence boundaries

The archive sequence in this particular source is normal xxd dump â†’ bzip2 â†’ gzip â†’ bzip2 â†’ tar â†’ one password-like member. The reusable guidance identifies/tests each actual layer and retains complete status instead of guessing the sequence. Both decompressors can pass noncompressed data through with force/stdout, and truncated input can emit bytes before failure. A successful xxd reverse exit or readable inner file does not certify full source coverage. The published source's passwords and callback addresses are omitted from runtime material.

curl config is CLI input, not a constrained URL list. Output options pair with URLs in order: in the owned current-version fixture, output from the config was selected and an appended -o report did not replace it. --get encodes data into the URL rather than ordinary POST form bytes; the server decides whether the decoded value executes. A -q first argument skips only default curlrc, leaving explicit -K active. FILE support and protocol policy are distinct from read/write permissions. The source shows group-writable config mode and a command that resets its content after a delay; these are consequential race/restoration conditions. Page 10 shows both the HTTP request and a callback id result with UID 0, but this is source evidence, not an authoring reproduction or an assumption from a root-owned filename.

The source's nc build is not supplied. Accepted OpenBSD-style guidance is explicitly conditional on installed help; no historical -lvp or shell-execution option is generalized across variants. rlwrap editing occurs locally. Python pty.spawn is Unix-only, returns wait-status bits and can continue after input EOF while its child waits. The selected Python server follows symlinks, so document-root selection alone is not confinement; directory indexes and no authentication matter when serving a transfer artifact.

Primary material reviewed through browser views: [curl config/GET/output/protocol manual](https://curl.se/docs/manpage.html), [bzip2 manual](https://sourceware.org/bzip2/manual/manual.html), [OpenBSD nc](https://man.openbsd.org/nc), [rlwrap upstream manual](https://github.com/hanslub42/rlwrap/blob/master/doc/rlwrap.man.in), [Python pty](https://docs.python.org/3.12/library/pty.html), [Python HTTP server](https://docs.python.org/3.12/library/http.server.html) and [OpenSSH scp](https://man.openbsd.org/scp). Applicable prior primary verification for xxd/file/tar/pspy/SSH/Nmap/Base64/Wget was retained. GNU gzip online manual/source refreshes failed; installed GNU gzip help and owned integrity/concatenation/force cases establish the options/results actually used. Failed refreshes and a non-source GitLab response are not claimed source reviews. No executable source downloads were saved.

### Checks, corrections and limits

All **16 focused package validators**, **nine existing packaging/checker tests** and **19 inert Windows checks** passed. Cases include complete layered hex/compression/tar recovery and source retention; truncated, wrong-format/force and concatenated compression outcomes; config output association, encoded GET fields, HTTP 404/body distinction, FILE transfer/policy; owned-server shutdown and passive bzip2 help. Versions: GNU gzip **1.14**, bzip2 **1.0.8**, xxd **2026-06-16**, file **5.48**, GNU tar **1.35**, curl **8.21.0/Schannel** and Python **3.12.10**, all on Windows. One embedded PTY Python program parsed as AST, without Unix execution. Before maintenance-record additions, repository checks passed for **341 Markdown files, 505 local links, 222 packages and 549 Bash blocks parsed only**. The changed bzip2 block was subsequently rechecked; commands were not executed by syntax checking. Catalogue membership is exact, unique and alphabetical; Git whitespace passed. Raw evidence lives in ignored `runs/curling-20261010/` (verified rerun in `verified/`) and the selected PDF work folder. Final post-record checks precede queue completion.

The initial Windows tar fixture failed because its C:/ drive-letter archive was interpreted as a remote host. Initial logs were preserved; the verified rerun selected the owned archive relative to its working directory, recovering the expected member. This fixture correction does not change the Linux procedure. A bzip2 --version probe also printed its license and compressed empty stdin: the runtime example was corrected to passive --help, confirmed to emit its version/help on stderr without compressed stdout. The changed block and package were revalidated after that correction; old outputs remain as evidence.

No Linux fixture or agent/harness evaluation. nc/rlwrap/PTY, scp/target SSH, actual pspy/cron/privileged config or Joomla/PHP execution were not run. **Domain knowledge and source corrections:** p2 calls the login test SQL injection, but the p3 driver trace and graph query identify Cypher. The p4 bypass returns a chosen SHA-1 value under the exact `hash` alias so this application's password comparison succeeds; `OR true` alone is not the whole model, and the preceding graph pattern still needs rows. SHA-1 here is source-specific, not Neo4j's universal authentication storage. The protected `/demo` result is source observation; our local fixtures do not authenticate that application.

P5's argument array deliberately selects `/bin/sh -c`, so Java argv alone does not remove its concatenated URL's shell boundary. `Mode.READ` limits graph operations, not OS/network effects. P6's `000neo4j` mixes failed-transfer write-out and subsequent stdout; it is neither one HTTP status nor proof that curl itself succeeded. The connect timeout is not a total child runtime bound. `/etc/passwd` account/home fields are meaningful; selecting the last one/two Bash-shell lines is not a general way to identify the service account. History supplies a setup-secret candidate, while separate SSH success establishes that particular account's observed reuse. Raw secret/flag values are not copied into runtime guidance or this report.

P7 incorrectly describes BBOT as a VivaGraphJS visualization tool: it is a reconnaissance engine with visualization output examples. Its preset caption uses `my_present.yml`, while p8 invokes `my_preset.yml`. The printed Python uses `def MyModule(BaseModule)` and top-level effects rather than a valid subclass; it lacks a coherent demonstrated handler lifecycle. Current upstream preloading rejects files lacking expected class metadata, and actual loading executes accepted files before locating their classes. Accordingly the shown flag-file result is recorded as source observation, not proof that this exact malformed snippet reached import or that its handler ran. The corrected diagnostic module is illustrative and AST-checked; no BBOT runtime reproduction is claimed. Source banner is **v2.1.0.4939rc**; current stable source/docs were checked, but exact historical release code was unavailable at the attempted raw release references. Installed-version adaptation remains necessary. Neither `--force` nor `--dry-run` is elevated authority; current CLI prepares modules before its dry-run scan-loop gate.
