**Source observations versus corrections:** pp4/7 show response-size outliers and later protected pages; error outliers are not all accepted credentials. P5's MySQL/cache comments motivate a cache hypothesis but do not establish freshness or the entire backend architecture. P8 attributes the broader symbol-filter claim to an IppSec video; the shown finite character list alone is not universal coverage. P9's filtered table includes a 500 anomaly, so HTTP success/failure must not be treated as raw TCP state. Its 11211 lead is followed by actual ASCII-protocol responses; a TCP bridge does not become UDP because a port-search screenshot mentions UDP.

P11's slab classes 16/26 and stock/users keys are this instance's allocation, not stable schema. Key enumeration can omit live items, and get can miss after expiry. The current protocol's older metadata-dump size description and current cachedump code differ; no fixed complete-dump guarantee is adopted. P12's replacement decodes only one entity; HTML length is not Memcached payload length, and quote-splitting pretty JSON is not a lossless account parser. The HTML/JSON glue is illustrative authored code, tested with public inert data; it did not decode the target's live cache.

P13 incorrectly calls CVE-2018-15473 a timing attack. The selected helper malforms a public-key request and classifies response/exception behavior; OpenSSH 7.8 separately describes parsing fixes and timing mitigations. Banner-only exposure and upstream's old affected-version header are not authoritative patch detection. P14 HashID prints several algorithms fitting the value, rather than uniquely identifying MD5. An offline Raw-MD5 match and the later observed selected SSH login are distinct results.

P15's `ldconfig -v` can mutate cache/links even when its output is piped into a directory-list filter; the nonmutating scan requires both `-N -X`. P16/19's directory-order explanation is conditional on the actual dependency/search/cache rules, not a universal `/tmp` precedence rule. P17's function-name explanation does not establish when the library is loaded: direct ELF dependencies can load before main. The command shown retrieves the executable; acquisition of the associated library is stated in prose, without a shown matching-library transfer command. P18's stack string must be connected to comparison operands and the branch, not accepted solely because the debugger displays it. Debugger/ldd evidence is not the actual setuid launch.

P19's C snippet omits proper declarations, error checks and valid portable argument vectors; `execve(..., NULL, NULL)` is a Linux allowance, not portable process guidance. The diagnostic preserves the real operational build/cache/load/call sequence while printing identity from the observed function. P20 provides the source's actual UID-zero shell observation; it was not reproduced here. Raw credentials, hashes and flags are not copied into runtime guidance or this report.

**Primary verification:** Wfuzz [form/cookie/request options and pacing](https://wfuzz.readthedocs.io/en/latest/user/basicusage.html) and [filter parser](https://github.com/xmendez/wfuzz/blob/master/src/wfuzz/ui/console/clparser.py); [curl encoding/cookie/timeout manual](https://curl.se/docs/manpage.html); Memcached [basic protocol](https://docs.memcached.org/protocols/basic/), [framing/crawler specification](https://github.com/memcached/memcached/blob/2d51e364799bc9698bd4b11728ea978cea12da6e/doc/protocol.txt) and [legacy cachedump implementation](https://github.com/memcached/memcached/blob/master/items.c); [jq selectors/type/argument semantics](https://jqlang.org/manual/), [Python HTML decoding](https://docs.python.org/3/library/html.html#html.unescape) and [Vim substitution flags](https://github.com/vim/vim/blob/master/runtime/doc/change.txt).

Read the [enumeration helper](https://github.com/Rhynorater/CVE-2018-15473-Exploit/blob/ae8cb417c4bf441cb36413a2f6d5669f61cbddce/sshUsernameEnumExploit.py), its unpinned Paramiko requirements and [OpenSSH 7.8 changes](https://www.openssh.org/txt/release-7.8). Read [PEDA's command guide](https://github.com/longld/peda) and pinned [context, argument and startup implementation](https://github.com/longld/peda/blob/84d38bda505941ba823db7f6c1bcca1e485a2d43/peda.py), plus GDB [explicit Python loading](https://sourceware.org/gdb/current/onlinedocs/gdb.html/Python-Commands.html)/[command files](https://sourceware.org/gdb/current/onlinedocs/gdb.html/Command-Files.html). Pinned helper/extension/protocol source was read in memory and metadata recorded, never imported/executed or saved as attack files on Windows. No PEDA/Paramiko installation occurred.

Verified ldconfig [option manual](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mkerrisk/man-pages/master/man8/ldconfig.8)/[glibc option implementation](https://github.com/bminor/glibc/blob/master/elf/ldconfig.c), [ldd resolution and execution warning](https://manpages.debian.org/bookworm/manpages/ldd.1.en.html), [loader search/secure mode](https://manpages.debian.org/bookworm/manpages/ld.so.8.en.html), [execve declarations/privilege suppression/NULL vectors](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mkerrisk/man-pages/master/man2/execve.2), GCC [PIC](https://gcc.gnu.org/onlinedocs/gcc/Code-Gen-Options.html)/[shared linking](https://gcc.gnu.org/onlinedocs/gcc/Link-Options.html), GNU [id implementation](https://github.com/coreutils/coreutils/blob/master/src/id.c) and [user-namespace semantics](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mkerrisk/man-pages/master/man7/user_namespaces.7). Some man7 pages/GNU HTML manual fetches failed; primary raw manual/upstream source and Debian's distributed manpages supplied the actual verification, not unseen pages. Existing relevant format/protocol documentation was reused for unchanged HashID/John/Masscan/Nmap and selected transfer/source tasks.

**Validation:** 23 focused frontmatter passes; first-150 discoverability and complete affected tasks/references reviewed. All 15 existing repository tests passed. Nine inert Windows checks passed: one-layer HTML/JSON preservation (including Unicode and a literal inner entity), malformed JSON and invalid UTF-8 rejected before output, an existing destination preserved, illustrative Memcached payload byte count, Windows curl query-space/port and cookie capture, HTTP 200 application denial with zero transfer status, and stopped owned loopback server. The inline converter passed Python AST parsing. Initial illustrative count assertion failed before any edit/acceptance; it was corrected to the actual 37-byte payload and verified. No target data/backend/credential tests or mocked attack runtime. Windows curl 8.21.0/Schannel was actually used against a byte/query echo fixture only.

Repository check: 370 Markdown files, 593 local links, 233 packages, 592 Bash blocks **syntax-parsed only**, no errors. Exact unique alphabetical catalogue and Git whitespace passed. After the main check, Vim's vi-implementation sentence/description received a final focused frontmatter check; shell examples unchanged. Structural shim validation is not a general YAML parser/schema pass. Checks are in ignored `work/overnight-ingest-2026-10-10/Dab/checks.json`; owned fixtures in ignored `runs/dab-20261010/`.

No Wfuzz/Jq/GDB/PEDA/compiler/loader/cache/helper execution, Memcached backend request, attack, target authentication, installation, Defender/system/power change, original deletion/move, commit/push or Linux worker evaluation. Binary paths and exact historical helper/runtime behavior remain the later image-pass scope; unavailable Linux execution did not disqualify the documented supported tasks. Burp/Cookie Manager and browser operations are GUI evidence; no unidentified CLI implementation is invented. Broader unprovided video claims and exact legacy-version reproduction are deliberately unclaimed, with operational lessons retained in the references. Dab's all-page review and source-to-task coverage are complete for this intake.

Dab final maintenance-record check: 370 Markdown files, 617 local links and 233 packages passed after saving intake/validation. The 592 syntax-checked Bash blocks were unchanged and were not executed. Catalogue, whitespace and source SHA-256 passed. Dab completed; DarkCorp.pdf remains pending for the next heartbeat.


## DarkCorp.pdf â€” continuation, 2026-10-10

**Coverage:** all 34 physical pages text-read (44,777 extracted page-text characters), rendered at 100 dpi and individually visually inspected, including browser/mail, SQL results, graph paths, token/task/configuration and final Group Policy screenshots. SHA-256 `d9f410e8f760fe82d690d20c12dea40901278fcaa5ea81f8d29eb4baaa1da065` matched before opening and at acceptance; 2,346,898 bytes. Official Hack The Box writeup, 17 October 2025, prepared by/machine author 0xEr3bus, Insane difficulty. Pypdf reported two invalid zero-offset pointers (objects 11/22); all pages extracted/rendered and were read visually, so these warnings were not treated as lost-page coverage. Poppler rendering completed. Original retained. Only this PDF was selected; no later source opened.

**Outcome, schema v0.8:** added 13 independent packages: **whoami, groups, ifconfig, less, klist, gpg, dnstool, printerbug, impacket-smbexec, inveigh, krbrelayx, credentialmanager, sharp-gpoabuse**. Similar existing tools did not disqualify discovery, and Windows programs used through a Linux worker's remote session remain supported. Enriched/conformed 18: **psql, impacket-gettgt, proxychains, python-http-server, curl, chisel, netexec, impacket-ntlmrelayx, certipy, bloodhound-python, ldbsearch, ssh, evil-winrm, john, impacket-ticketer, impacket-secretsdump, strings, sudo**. Reviewed five relevant packages unchanged: **nmap, hostname, wget, nc, id**. These 36 packages are the actually reviewed set, not a wholesale library migration. Catalogue now contains 246 accepted independent packages.

The affected tasks were reconciled with prior guidance rather than appended as a source transcript. Substantial branches now load conditionally: PostgreSQL server files/WAL hooks; contact-mail/browser testing; bounded POST collection with exact package-local helper; normal Chisel callback direction; LDAP protection/AD CS endpoint selection; selected DNS relay actions; Kerberos SMB-to-enrollment; shadow credentials/UPN changes; enterprise-principal requests; SSH GSSAPI mapping/delegation; remote SAM acquisition; generated service-ticket use; and selected-user scheduled-task credential recovery. Existing offline hive/NTDS, certificate enrollment, query exports, SSH/key/configuration and other unrelated branches remain. Early contracts/routes, selected completion evidence and first-150 discoverability were reviewed. The strings entrypoint lacked an early activation contract and was corrected; sudo now connects a matching policy to an actual selected permitted execution and identity result.

### Source observations, corrections and limits

Pp3â€“9 establish source-specific recipient routing, vulnerable HTML rendering, selected mail/reset results and an Analytics vhost. Recipient control alone is not a universal IDOR classification. CVE-2024-42009's post-sanitization body-to-div transformation matters; an HTML content label or HTTP send result alone does not establish script execution. The source's 100-UID loop/custom collector is not copied as a default bulk recovery ritual. Same-origin fetch and later cross-origin `no-cors` sending have different response-reading rules. Base64/eval wrappers were read as text, never executed. The source's five-minute reset claim is application evidence, not a universal token lifetime.

Pp10â€“13 show PostgreSQL 15.10, SQL superuser and large-object server-file operations. Their service OS identity is postgres, not root. Exporting complete configuration bytes is an actual server write; it does not become a read-only task because a shell payload is omitted. The archive mechanism remains documented, with a reviewed copy/WAL command and actual result requirement. `archive_mode`/`wal_level` are startup-only; a true `pg_reload_conf` signals reload, not restart-only application or command execution. `archive_command` additionally depends on an active archive mode and library policy. UI HTML encoding and SQL context are preserved without treating a short injection prefix as a universal carrier. No runtime reproduction of the source's claimed reload-only transition is asserted.

Pp14â€“20 require internal vantage. `whoami & hostname` backgrounds one command and does not reliably order the identities. The source's transferred Nmap 6.49BETA1 also warns that `nmap-payloads` is absent; that affects supported probes/data and is not evidence the discovered host list was fabricated. A private key's absence does not rule out a known-passphrase symmetric GPG backup; the source's categorical rejection was too broad. A SQLAlchemy Python file does not prove Django. LDAP bind success, DNS node permissions, directory quota, graph rights and certificate endpoint/authentication are independent. Current NetExec no longer executes `ldap-checker`; its stub directs users to the native banner, whose labels also differ from old output.

Pp20â€“24 contain a concrete tunnel discrepancy: prose describes forwarding port 80, while the command's receiver port is 8080. Normal Chisel remotes bind on the client and reach the destination from the server; source `0.0.0.0` is not a reliable final receiver address. The new reference names the actual listener/destination separately. An application error can coexist with received integrated authentication. NetNTLMv2 capture is not an NT hash. Ntlmrelayx's `--no-validate-privs` does not suppress ACL action; the selected DNS procedure explicitly disables ACL/domain-admin/dump actions. An NTLM HTTP challenge failure does not prove PKU2U or automatic Kerberos compatibility. The marshalled DNS suffix's byte spelling/63-octet label bound, SPN-versus-connection name, RPC-denied-yet-callback possibility, AD CS template/principal and certificate identity are retained. An `unknown...pfx` filename is not the authenticated account.

Pp24â€“30 distinguish local SAM Administrator from AD Administrator, CIFS service-ticket authority from replication, SCM SYSTEM from the authenticating user's store and WinRM network-logon 1312 from empty global stores. The source's credential-manager/password-change explanation is not accepted as a general guarantee of secret availability or destruction. Selected `Get-StoredCredential` uses CredRead, while no-target enumeration uses CredEnumerate; default credential type is Generic. Existing task logon types have different credential requirements, so no universal requirement to know a cleartext password for every task is imposed. The selected output path/ACL and exact original script/task state are required; the source's broadly readable Tasks output is not the default. Shadow auto restoration is verified, not assumed after an exception. An enterprise TGT is KDC-issued, and bare UPN/name mapping does not turn every Linux service into the comparison account. Windows SSPI/SID/PAC and particular Linux GSSAPI/NSS application authorization differ. Portable SSH `-K` delegates credentials as well as enabling GSSAPI.

Pp31â€“34 retain the actual root RunAs observation and its separate process identity, SSSD configuration, cached verifier and GPO write. LDB cache files are databases, not logs; `cache_credentials` is not a guarantee every GSSAPI login stored a password. `simple_allow_groups` depends on the selected access provider. A nearby strings username cannot assign a `$6$` verifier; the selected DN/attribute preserves that mapping. Offline recovery is not proof of a current AD credential. GPO links/filters determine fanout beyond the connection host; DC refresh defaults are not the member-client 90-minute generalization. The operator's `.adm` identity and the account actually added in the source are kept distinct. Policy write, client application and fresh enabled token membership remain separate, with cleanup accounting for membership that removing a setting might not undo.

### Verification and validation

Verified relevant CLI/behavior definitions against primary sources: [Roundcube/Sonar vulnerability research](https://www.sonarsource.com/blog/government-emails-at-risk-critical-cross-site-scripting-vulnerability-in-roundcube-webmail/); [PostgreSQL 15 large objects](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/15/lo-funcs.html), [WAL settings](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/15/runtime-config-wal.html) and [reload/configuration functions](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/15/functions-admin.html); [GnuPG controls/status](https://www.gnupg.org/documentation/manuals/gnupg26/gpg.1.html); [Chisel direction/authentication](https://github.com/jpillora/chisel#usage); [Python HTTP APIs](https://docs.python.org/3/library/http.server.html), [curl encoding/status](https://curl.se/docs/manpage.html) and [browser request modes](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Request/mode).

Relay/name-mapping semantics used primary [Synacktiv Kerberos SMB research](https://www.synacktiv.com/en/publications/relaying-kerberos-over-smb-using-krbrelayx) and [Pentest Partners mixed-stack analysis](https://www.pentestpartners.com/security-blog/a-broken-marriage-abusing-mixed-vendor-kerberos-stacks/), plus upstream krbrelayx, Impacket, Certipy, NetExec and Inveigh implementations. ### Observations, corrections and domain depth

The source shows a Grafana 8.0.0 footer and selected file responses. The vendor advisory gives affected/fixed branches, while the versioned plugin handler resolves a registered plugin before joining/opening the requested path. Plugin absence, path absence, proxy normalization, service identity and the actual backend/path therefore matter. The database's HTTP transfer completion is historical evidence, not proof of a coherent live SQLite snapshot; the accepted reference retains the WAL/sidecar limit and rejects a login/error body masquerading as a database. The common packaged database path is an acquisition lead, not a guessed binary path for our controlled image.

Grafana 8.0.0's producer explicitly uses SHA-256 PBKDF2, **10,000 rounds and 50 derived bytes**, then hexadecimal output. Its literal salt is not a hex-salt field, and rands is not an input to that function. The source's converter uses `sys.exit` without importing sys in its malformed-hex branch; it was read, not executed/copied. PDF line wrapping must not become verifier bytes. The existing iamaldi converter hardcodes the cost, splits on commas and prints banners/suggested commands; its output option and a delimiter-preserving fallback retain meaningful operational decisions. Hashcat's reviewed parser compares a 16-byte prefix, so the accepted procedure independently checks the full original verifier before attributing a recovered candidate. It does not infer current OS/account access from an application-row match.

The source prose overstates adding privileges to a running container. `docker exec --privileged` applies to the new process, and versioned Moby sets process capabilities while retaining the container's AppArmor context; this is not a general device/mount-policy bypass. A container UID-zero command, an accessible block device, the actual source filesystem and successful host login are different evidence. The accepted procedure retains mounting/file-change tasks, with observed device/type/namespace, rather than assuming `/dev/sda1` everywhere. Read-only ext3/ext4 can replay a dirty journal; `ro,noload` is filesystem-specific and can expose unreplayed inconsistency. Existing writable views/live writers and the task's actual write scope must be considered.

The p5 authorized-key `>` operation replaces existing keys. The accepted SSH reference instead preserves the actual file/metadata, appends the selected public key and verifies enrollment separately from login. A writable filesystem does not establish server AuthorizedKeysFile/StrictModes/PermitRootLogin policy or host identity. Root key login can be permitted by prohibit-password but rejected by other effective policy; no server setting change is a default recovery step. Private keys remain on their client. Cleanup reconciles only the added key if intervening legitimate changes exist.

### Consequential observations and corrections

- A helpdesk-generated domain reply address exposes a mail route associated with a ticket, not a fully controlled employee mailbox. The source's p6/p7 screenshots use different ticket identifiers; the reported historical narrative is not proof of one continuously correlated request. The new procedure requires the actual owned ticket/message/signup association. P7's verification email is not, by itself, evidence of a staff-issued invitation. Domain verification, account creation and team/channel authorization are separate boundaries; broad claims about all helpdesks or cloud services were not promoted into universal product behavior.
- P9's MySQL-driver DSN names TCP loopback while the displayed bare mysql login can use a Unix socket. JSON `\u0026` must be decoded before DSN interpretation; a config copy can be overridden/stale. `AtRestEncryptKey` is not the SQL password or a bcrypt salt. P10's screenshot shows **MariaDB 10.3.27** and a full **`$2a$10$` bcrypt** record. Historical Mattermost v5.30.0 source verifies bcrypt cost 10, but does not identify the PDF installation's exact Mattermost version. Current vendor documentation describes progressive PBKDF2 migration from v11.0; select from actual records/version rather than assuming every Mattermost password is bcrypt.
- Hashcat `--stdout` generates candidates and checks no hash. The seed/rule clue is narrower than all possible password variants. Rule duplicates/rejection, file encoding and stacking Sucrack rewrites can change the tested space. App user root, app roles, SQL login and OS root are different identities. A recovered application candidate becomes a reuse hypothesis until the selected service/OS session is independently established.
- P14's Sucrack **1.2.3** screenshot warns that both `-a` and `-s` are unavailable without `--enable-statistics`, then prints a candidate success line. Upstream guards confirm that these warnings can coexist with continued checking. `-s` is display refresh, not an authentication delay/time cap; `-w` is concurrency, not an attempt count. The copied 20-worker/rewrite choices were replaced with deliberate account-policy/budget decisions. The source's later Sucrack output alone does not demonstrate a second independently verified root session.
- Sucrack uses a pseudoterminal, invokes su with a success-marker command and parses strings; zero status or a success-looking line is weaker than independently confirmed selected-account access. Local PAM delay/lockout/expiration, extra prompts, locale/implementation differences and restricted shells can break that parser or an exhaustion claim. Environment marker/path overrides use observed values; no deployed path was guessed and no protection/configuration change is a recovery prerequisite.
- A successful configure/make or a leftover executable does not establish configured features/new revision/runtime success. GNU make dry-run/question modes have executable exceptions; they are not trust checks. ZIP add/update can retain stale members, and Unix symlink storage is a deliberate transfer choice. Archive CRC does not establish source trust or safe extraction. Transfer/build/install/account-testing results are separated without removing the supported procedures.

### Primary verification and actual validation

Reviewed applicable primary Sucrack [README](https://github.com/hemp3l/sucrack/blob/master/README.md), [CLI](https://github.com/hemp3l/sucrack/blob/master/src/sucrack.c), [su helper](https://github.com/hemp3l/sucrack/blob/master/src/su.c) and [configure feature definition](https://github.com/hemp3l/sucrack/blob/master/configure.ac); upstream [util-linux su manual](https://github.com/util-linux/util-linux/blob/master/login-utils/su.1.adoc); GNU make [execution-mode manual](https://www.gnu.org/software/make/manual/html_node/Instead-of-Execution.html); the packaged upstream [Info-ZIP 3.0 manual](https://manpages.debian.org/bookworm/zip/zip.1.en.html); Hashcat [CLI help](https://github.com/hashcat/hashcat/blob/master/docs/hashcat-help.md) and [rule guide](https://hashcat.net/wiki/doku.php?id=rule_based_attack); historical Mattermost [config model](https://github.com/mattermost/mattermost/blob/v5.30.0/model/config.go) and [user/password model](https://github.com/mattermost/mattermost/blob/v5.30.0/model/user.go); current vendor [authentication](https://docs.mattermost.com/administration-guide/configure/authentication-configuration-settings) / [database configuration](https://docs.mattermost.com/administration-guide/configure/environment-configuration-settings); [Go MySQL-driver DSN](https://github.com/go-sql-driver/mysql#dsn-data-source-name); and osTicket [email configuration](https://docs.osticket.com/en/latest/Admin/Emails/Emails.html) / [email settings](https://docs.osticket.com/en/latest/Admin/Emails/Settings.html). ### Consequential observations and qualifications

- The p6 paused arguments have pointer difference **48**, and pp8–9 provide a generated 113-character Metasploit pattern whose observed adjacent-field fragment starts at **48**. This corroborates that particular display-field overwrite; it is not a universal offset or native saved-return-address/control-flow result. UTF-8 byte length, temporary ccall stack strings, termination, transformation and DOM escaping remain separate boundaries. P7's ordinary script username is escaped; data reaching the date field crosses a different sink. Client maxlength is not server validation.
- P10's initial GET is from the operator's own browser; p11's later headless-renderer GET follows the report action. Source peer/user-agent differences support a different visitor but do not independently prove administrator identity. Module retrieval differs from execution: the raw Ncat listener supplied no valid module response, while Python supplied CORS and JavaScript content. Existing CSP/mixed-content/MIME/CORS requirements remain browser-side constraints; a curl success cannot verify them.
- The source's p11 prose names port 300, while its request code uses **3000**. Its base64 query construction does not explicitly percent-encode the value, and direct btoa has byte-character constraints. These historical observations were not promoted into universal robust transfer recipes. The improved branch selects complete bytes/outer encoding and leaves truncated logs incomplete. HttpOnly prevents direct cookie reading; the source rides the browser's same-origin session and reads pages, not a decrypted/stolen cookie.
- Hidden report_log is caller-controlled; the token is current session-bound CSRF data, not an administrator grant. Routes/controllers plus observed file response support the file/process chain. `/proc/self/cwd` belongs to the server process opening that path. A basename applied only to the outgoing download name does not constrain the earlier file read. Unqualified open can resolve to Ruby Kernel.open in relevant versions, where a leading pipe invokes a subprocess; the actual binding/version matters, and File.open is different. Rails redirect_to does not return automatically; later rendering errors can occur after an action. The runtime reference documents the selected process check rather than substituting only source inspection.
- P18 shows users(username,password_digest,role,...) and complete **$2a$12$ bcrypt** records. An application's administrator role is not OS UID 0. P19's selected account has real UID 999 and groups including openmediavault-engined/config; those current groups and local socket access are relevant, not a generic full sudo grant. No actual password/identity was recovered or authenticated here.
- The source does not identify the exact OpenMediaVault version. Verified **upstream 6.x** CLI/schema/backend contracts align with its displayed operations: -u admin supplies an administrator RPC context, the local engined Unix socket has a group/mode boundary, Apt.install accepts a string array and runs a background apt-get installation. A method inventory or one read does not guarantee every mutation is permitted. Filename shell quoting does not remove the separate privilege boundary of caller-chosen package hooks.
- The source's p21 successful RPC returns a JSON string naming a background status file, then separately shows a setuid mode and `id` with real UID 999/effective UID 0 after Bash -p. The receipt alone is not installation completion. Upstream Exec.getOutput uses filename/pos and optional length≥1024, reports running/pendingOutput, throws backend errors and consumes finished fully-read status. **Exec.isRunning can also consume finished status**, so the reference collects getOutput directly when logs matter. This verified recovery detail extends the source, without claiming it was executed on its target.
- Dpkg-deb builds/inspects an archive; only the installer invokes hooks with its available authority. A controlled postinst marker/identity output is documented as an alternative capability probe without permanent setuid system-file alteration; installation still changes package state. Native payloads need actual architecture, and root-owner-group changes recorded archive data ownership, not builder identity. Partial install/configuration, dependencies, locks and script stages need separate state/error handling before retry. -p preserves an already supplied effective identity; mode alone is weaker than actual identity/capability evidence.

**Consequential source corrections.** RIP is the instruction pointer, not a “Return Instruction Pointer”; assigning it does not build a call frame or demonstrate normal input, stack corruption or elevated identity. The hosted page's `document.cookie` belongs to its own origin. Its empty callback does not prove Django cookie security or same-origin XSS. Opener abuse from an owned external page need not involve same-origin XSS; modern implicit noopener/COOP/browser context must be observed. A page fetch, navigation, simulated POST and original-service authentication establish different effects.

The p14 screenshot identifies **Django1.6.11/Python2.7.18** and a Sentry environment. Django1.6 defaults to JSON; the clipped debug settings alone do not independently prove PickleSerializer. The displayed explicitly pickle-based signing procedure and p16 effect support this case, not every exposed key. Preserve the effective serializer, signing key, exact cookie name/domain/path, namespace and timestamp-age behavior; the prose/editor naming/quotes are not a portable cookie contract. The p16 `nc` banner identifies **Nmap Ncat7.92**; the remote client's nc implementation is not shown and needs its own help/manual. Source callback plus `id` reports UID33/www-data, not OS root. `is_staff` and an email/first-name clue likewise do not define a Unix identity. The full Django record uses a **literal salt and 32-byte base64 digest**, not mode10900's generic base64-salt layout.

The Rust description incorrectly adds a 16-byte IV to a 16-byte key to claim a 32-byte key: this is **AES-128**, with an independent full counter block. The p22–23 prose IV has only 30 hex characters/15 bytes; p24's solver contains the complete 16-byte input. Recover missing bytes from the artifact/verified capture rather than guessing. A 32-byte comparison is a byte length, not necessarily 32 displayed characters. Decompiled ABI arguments/enum widths are hypotheses, not direct high-level parameter count or key-size proof. Preserve rejection when length **or** bytes mismatch, rather than inheriting the prose's weak “and” explanation. CTR has no padding/tag; re-encrypting with the same wrong key also matches its ciphertext, so a round trip is not independent correctness evidence. The authenticator's public-key prompt denotes a persistent operation; its exact write path/append/replace policy is not visible enough here to invent a universal `authorized_keys` location.

Pip's [interpreter guide](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/user_guide/), [install controls](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/cli/pip_install/), [secure installs](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/topics/secure-installs/) and [Python2 removal](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/news/#v21-0) were read. ### Page coverage and source corrections

Page 1 supplies provenance/synopsis. Page 2 shows 22/80/8000 discovery, HTTP redirect/hosts plumbing and OpenSSH/Apache banners. Pages 3–5 establish the SSH-chat application, actual legacy option and historical help/command names; current upstream differs, so a historical command list is not a universal contract. Page 6 shows pets virtual-host and .git discovery; the prose overstates redirect-based validity and uses a 200 blacklist that hides all 200 routes. Page 7 shows downloader checkout and the HTTP handler routes. Page 8 shows the JSON species flow into explicit `sh -c` and `CombinedOutput` plus the listener. Page 9's prose quotes the Content-Type header name incorrectly; the screenshot uses the correct header and demonstrates a callback/UID 1000, private-key acquisition, nano/chmod and a separate SSH login. Its key display contains omitted material; no missing key bytes were invented.

Page 10 shows the loopback chat/InfluxDB lead and ping header 1.7.5. Pages 11–12 show the online JWT editor and a candidate username/one-year exp/empty signing secret, followed by database-query acceptance. The source guesses an administrative user from chat context; the reusable procedure records the selected metastore-username evidence or explicit single-user hypothesis and distinguishes disabled-auth controls. Pages 13–14 show devzat's `user` measurement and returned positional values, then selected su/id UID 1001. The writeup's “table” wording was corrected to measurement; application enabled/password fields are not OS-account state or current credential proof. Raw secrets are not embedded in runtime skills/report examples.

Page 15 shows a loopback 8443 netstat row and two source backups. Page 16 shows an actual RSA 3072 key-generation banner, key enrollment and SCP retrieval. Page 17 shows unzip and a nonrecursive diff revealing root-level source changes; it does not cover common subdirectories. Page 18 shows the custom guarded file handler, cwd/path joining, stat/open/scanner and changed loopback port. Source prose has a malformed traversal segment (`../.../`) while the screenshot shows ordinary parent segments; the skill teaches actual cwd/path interpretation instead of copying either string. Page 19 shows the historical custom `/file` command, system-prefixed key lines and a separate successful OS session/id UID 0. These are source observations, not reproduced target results or a universal Devzat vulnerability.

Fixture history retains that failure/correction, not just the eventual pass. Temporary server and owned files were cleaned up; no source key was processed or used.

No Linux netstat/chmod/nano, full jwt_tool/InfluxDB/Gobuster/git-dumper/Go/Devzat runtime, target authentication/request, shell payload/callback, sudo/PAM/key enrollment or worker evaluation occurred. Live application identity, exact deployed build/guard/cwd, permissions, callback route and record handling remain necessary runtime inputs. ### Complete page coverage and corrections

Page 1: synopsis/provenance, SSTI → web/adm → misplaced log candidate → root Splunk service path. Page 2: full-port/service scan and screenshots showing 22, 80 and 8089, service/certificate clues. Page 3: discovered hostname, nano hosts edit and alternate login application. Page 4: browser registration/account expiry, stored post and commented /archive lead; browser blankness versus XML bytes. Page 5: title in RSS plus engine-decision chart and initial `${7*7}` probe. Page 6: literal initial probe, curly arithmetic output 49 and next string-multiplication probe; its pasted XML contains duplicated/misplaced closing tags, so it is not a reliable complete XML parser fixture. Page 7: `7777777`, Jinja hypothesis, warning-class object-graph command, callback id web/adm and Python PTY. Page 8: passwd rows omitted from extraction, groups prose, recursive grep/request candidate, plain su and screenshot id. Page 9: splunkd root/process-runner screenshot, source clone/folder and default-credential attempt. Page 10: Unauthorized screenshot, selected credential accepted, bundle server/install message and FIFO callback. Page 11: callback/no-tty warning and observed root id. All screenshots read; no partial extraction marked completed.

Corrected the source's broad SSTI explanation: a value passed into a fixed template is normally data; user-controlled text compiled as template source crosses the evaluation boundary. Default Jinja multiplication corroborates, but does not uniquely identify, the engine. Arithmetic success neither proves OS execution nor defeats a configured sandbox. The warning-class path is loaded-object/private-attribute dependent, not a stable subclass index; the retained identity probe narrows the selected class and separates output/error/child lifetime. Repeated archive reads can rerun a stored payload. Browser labels do not prove POST field names, CSRF shape or curl use.

Corrected broad group/service claims: adm permits many logs according to actual filesystem access, not all /var/log or root authority; a root-owned daemon screenshot is separate from an available splunk passwd account. A POST request line can expose a query value even though its body is separate. HTTP 500 does not establish reset success or rollback. Source plaintext candidates are excluded from reusable examples; raw evidence stays ignored. OS-login acceptance and Splunk acceptance remain distinct.

Corrected the writeup's default-attempt interpretation: Unauthorized does not prove the default password was changed, because remote-login policy can reject unchanged defaults. Current UF9.1+ local/UDS management behavior is not silently projected onto the historical exposed instance. The reviewed script treats only initial HTTP401 as auth failure and prints install/removal success after error responses; those messages alone prove neither action. Script stdout goes toward indexing, not back to the CLI; its id install screenshot lacks independent id output. The later callback id is stronger execution evidence. Installation/update/script scheduling, RETURN cleanup and actual app/child removal must remain separate. Linux shebang/.sh and fresh FIFO adaptations are author-added supported procedures, not source screenshots or executed trials.

### Complete page coverage and evidence corrections

Page 1: synopsis/provenance, exposed Git → recovered credential → Backdrop admin code installation → web worker → OS reuse → sudo Bee evaluation → setuid Bash. Page 2: full/selected port scan screenshots, hostname setup, virtualenv/pip and actual git-dumper command. Page 3: sanitization/automatic checkout then restore/settings, loopback MySQL DSN, login-throttling discussion, alias-pattern reasoning and ffuf invocation. Page 4: same-size 403 candidate responses, separate admin login/dashboard screenshot, testing.info and install-route distinction. Page 5: screenshots of missing ZIP support and catalog DNS error, accepted manual archive formats, published local generator and tar creation. Page 6: manual route `/?q=admin/installer/manual`, selected tar upload and authorization success screenshot with separate enable links; direct PHP output **www-data**. Page 7: Bash callback, nc, PTY/TERM/local stty, passwd/SSH account context and explicit sshpass/SSH identity. Page 8: password-authenticated sudo policy, Bee root flag/full eval reasoning and actual shell_exec call. Page 9: output **UID0**, copied Bash/setuid mode and later **real UID1001/effective UID0**, with supplementary groups still1001. All nine renders read individually; no extracted-text-only completion claim.

Corrected the source's inferred boundaries while retaining its operational depth. Backdrop's default `accounts/[user:name]` alias pattern is configurable and sanitized, while q handles routing without clean URL rewriting; not every missing/403 alias means the same thing. The referenced BackDropScan's 403 heuristic is reviewed as such, not promoted into authoritative user validation. Same-size forbidden pages alone do not settle it; a separate authenticated result does. A settings DSN's database root/password/loopback address do not identify an OS account or external MySQL endpoint. The page7 prompt is already labeled johncusack before the explicit SSH transcript; it is not independent proof of an unshown transition.

Corrected generator/upload/process interpretation: EDB52021 is **Ahmet Ümit BAYRAM's** authenticated Backdrop1.27.1 generator, published 19 May2024 with 27 April2024 source date. Reviewed for behavior without download/import/execution; it creates local executable PHP, not a remote exploitation request. The PDF prose calls it a reverse shell, but the endpoint calls system on GET cmd and the later Bash command creates the callback. Missing server ZIP support and catalog DNS failure are different prerequisites; tar fallback retains the actual module tree. Install, enable, direct-file execution and web-process authority remain separate, deployment-specific results. No multipart field or universal vulnerability invented from screenshots.

Corrected Bee/identity interpretation: eval/php-script perform a full trusted application bootstrap; recovered untrusted code should not be bootstrapped just to inspect a credential. Source uses shell_exec despite prose mentioning system. system can supply a child exit status; shell_exec stdout/empty result alone is ambiguous. Bee's reviewed eval callback catches ParseError; php-script silently returns for a missing file, so zero/no output cannot prove the task. CLI PHP settings/process can differ from web PHP. A path-only sudo grant under the shown policy permits chosen arguments, but exact executable/RunAs/wrapper/authentication constraints matter. Copying as root normally supplies the new file owner; u+s changes a bit, and Bash -p preserves an effective identity already supplied by execution. It does not convert supplementary groups, guarantee host-wide authority or bypass mount/no_new_privs conditions. Fresh-artifact/child restoration stays part of the selected procedure. Original source passwords/flag values are excluded from reusable guidance.

Incidental ls/pwd/cat file-navigation/display and cut/tr/sed text plumbing are grouped, as permitted by the coverage rules: no substantive utility-specific procedure beyond locating/displaying this identified vault or formatting the Nmap list is extracted here. The supported acquisition/complete bytes/original preservation task is retained in pswm, shell context in Bash and reliable service selection in Nmap/grep. Burp browser/proxy/Intruder screenshots are GUI evidence; no GUI workflow invented as a Linux CLI package. SecLists provides selected special-character/password wordlists, not an invoked installer or autonomous spray procedure. No meaningful demonstrated task was rejected for overlapping tools, short invocation, authoring execution restrictions or absent Linux fixtures.

Verified primary semantics: [curl multiple URLs](https://curl.se/docs/httpscripting.html#multiple), [manpage](https://curl.se/docs/manpage.html) and [URL syntax](https://curl.se/docs/url-syntax.html); PHP [intval](https://www.php.net/manual/en/function.intval.php), [integer filters](https://www.php.net/manual/en/filter.constants.validation.php), [escapeshellcmd](https://www.php.net/manual/en/function.escapeshellcmd.php) and [exec](https://www.php.net/manual/en/function.exec.php); [Debian traditional nc](https://manpages.debian.org/unstable/netcat-traditional/nc.traditional.1.en.html), [actual netcat source](https://salsa.debian.org/debian/netcat/-/blob/master/netcat.c) and [OpenBSD nc](https://man.openbsd.org/nc); [hostname manual](https://manpages.debian.org/bookworm/hostname/hostname.1.en.html). Traditional nc's GAPING_SECURITY_HOLE build branch runs its -e program after connection before testing whether -z suppresses ordinary shoveling. OpenBSD -e is a TLS-name check, not execute; verify actual implementation rather than assuming all nc commands share semantics.

PDF hyperlinks identify [pswm](https://github.com/Julynx/pswm) and [SecLists special characters](https://github.com/danielmiessler/SecLists/blob/master/Fuzzing/special-chars.txt). pswm's [reviewed implementation](https://github.com/Julynx/pswm/blob/main/pswm), version1.5 by Julio Cabria, establishes existing-XDG selection/fallback, module-level directory creation, authentication before argument handling, native read/help rewrites, three-failure delete-reset offer, leading-dash parser behavior, duplicate/invalid/tab-row treatment and reserved master record. Useful retrieval is documented without importing the application for its decrypt helper or touching the original. Preserve attribution; authored procedures do not embed upstream application source.

[cryptocode API](https://github.com/gdavid7/cryptocode) and [implementation](https://github.com/gdavid7/cryptocode/blob/main/cryptocode.py) verify Base64 ciphertext/salt/nonce/tag ordering, scrypt N16384/r8/p1/dklen32 and AES-GCM authentication. Current raw source checks structure/strict Base64 and separates invalid data from some runtime exceptions; cached/published legacy implementation catches errors more broadly and returns False. Neither behavior is assumed for an unknown installed version. Empty plaintext is distinct from False; a False return is not universally proof of a wrong password. URL/byte/SHA snapshots for pswm, cryptocode and traditional nc are in ignored Down/checks.json. Executable upstream source read in memory only, never saved/imported/executed; no Defender bypass or raw-source restoration.

**Sixteen focused basic validators**, first150 discoverability routes and whole affected contexts passed. Existing validation-deps shim verifies basic frontmatter/naming, not complete YAML/spec compliance. Unique alphabetical README membership matches **265 packages**; **19 existing repository tests passed**, including package independence. No checker/test implementation changed. Broad check: **469 Markdown files, 927 local links, 756 Bash blocks parsed only**, no example execution. Python offline heredoc AST/compile passed without import/run. Whitespace and source/runtime hashes passed; final maintenance-link counts below.

**Fourteen owned inert Windows checks passed** with Python3.12.14, curl8.21.0 Schannel and installed cryptography50.0.2: nine actual curl transfer/form checks (two-URL body concatenation, initial malformed/last successful status0, fail-early status3, protocol denial status1, one malformed space-containing argument, encoded space, literal plus, inert option-text field and worker-local FILE capture); four public AES-GCM/envelope checks (same-format roundtrip, wrong trimmed candidate InvalidTag, altered tag InvalidTag and truncated-envelope structural detection); one original marker-integrity check. The mock only receives literal fields, never executes PHP/nc/commands/authentication. Cryptography tests format facts on public owned markers; **pswm/cryptocode are not installed/executed**, and this fixture is not a substitute library distributed to workers. No fixture failure; server and temporary-directory cleanup verified.

No verified Linux fixture/Pi-worker evaluation, pswm/cryptocode/PHP/nc runtime, target scan/login, callback/PTY/sudo/root execution. No attack, installation, system/power/Defender change, original move/delete, commit/push or guessed binary placement. Backend normal-route construction not fully recovered, target's exact netcat/PHP/cryptocode builds unknown, GUI-specific replay state and future image paths remain environment facts to verify; generic Gopher support is not a demonstrated protocol workflow. Only Down advances this queue; Delegate stays deferred by user instruction, coverage repairs and CrossFit2 remain complete.

## Present inbox completion — 2026-10-11

Detailed reasons are recorded in their entries above.

The source-to-task dispositions above separately account for demonstrated tools, already covered tasks, new/enriched procedures and concrete missing-fact deferrals. Exploitation, credential replay and intrusion chains were read and explicitly excluded from new operational packaging. Similar coverage in another tool did not suppress useful independent discovery. Name-only, GUI-only or unidentified implementation cases were not turned into invented CLI workflows.

### Validation and remaining environment work

- **19 existing unit tests passed**, including independent copies of all 290 packages, capture overwrite/failure/timeout cases and link/scaffold checks. The first attempt encountered Windows sandbox permissions in the default temporary parent; rerunning with process-local TEMP/TMP/TMPDIR under owned `runs/` succeeded. That temporary parent is empty after cleanup.
- **92 skill-creator quick validations passed** with the existing local PyYAML 6.0.3 dependency; no dependency was installed. First-150-line discovery and full affected entrypoint/task review covered all 49 changed packages and selected resources. The mechanical route audit confirms early activation and local resource routes; it is neither a universal harness limit nor proof of worker adherence.
- Repository check passed with **510 Markdown files, 993 local links, 290 packages and all 817 Bash blocks parsed**, before this completion footer. Bash parsing executes no example commands. Initial restricted MSYS startup failed with NtCreateDirectoryObject/access-denied; the inspected syntax-only checker succeeded through an approved execution exception. Final record/link/whitespace checks are recorded in [validation](validation.md#present-pdf-inbox-continuation--2026-10-11).
- **56 inert local fixture cases passed**: Node numeric types/bounds (15); SQLite metadata/absence (7); Git complete/incremental bundle recovery and object/tree/blob distinctions (8); Python distribution metadata and owned post-mortem failure (3); OpenSSL identity export/integrity and ASN.1 truncation (5); Bash status grammar (13), tr byte preservation (2) and exact mktemp creation/failure/cleanup (3). Fixtures use only newly created local public-marker data, never source programs, target accounts or payloads. Git fixture commits occur only in an ignored disposable repository under `runs/`; library history is unchanged. Test certificates were never installed as trust or used against a service.

Fixture environment: Windows, Python **3.12.14**, SQLite engine **3.53.1** through Python (not the sqlite3 CLI), Node **24.19.0**, Git **2.55.0.windows.5**, OpenSSL **3.5.7**, Git Bash **5.3.15(2)** and GNU coreutils tr/mktemp **8.32**. The first OpenSSL fixture startup hit the same sandbox MSYS restriction; the approved rerun then exposed an overly narrow fixture assertion for Git's actual “prerequisite commits” diagnostic. Only that fixture expectation was corrected, and the full suite passed in a fresh directory. Neither failure required changing accepted command guidance.

No controlled Linux image or worker trial was available. Linux capabilities/ACLs, device/FUSE/NFS mappings, capture privileges, X11 selection, service-specific authentication/transport and tools absent here remain documentation-reviewed with explicit prerequisites, rather than runtime-proven. The later on-box pass must establish actual binaries, relevant installed options and image facts in the package's own references. Passing these fixtures does not establish those conditions, original CTF outcomes or agent usefulness.

Local evidence: source extraction/render/review records and the queue/baseline audit are under ignored `work/intake-2026-10-10/` and `work/overnight-ingest-2026-10-10/`; test logs, route/quick-validation results and runtime SHA-256 records are under ignored `runs/intake-2026-10-11/`. Baseline checks found no unexpected file change or deletion; prior uncommitted unrelated contributions, source originals, authoring directives and the assessment methodology remain intact. Changes are left for Git review; no library commit, push or publication was performed.

Final maintenance-record check: **510 Markdown / 995 local links / 290 packages**, no errors; exact catalogue and whitespace checks passed. All **817 unchanged shell blocks** remain covered by the completed syntax-only pass. The completion prose adds no command blocks and does not alter the accepted runtime packages. The 41 present sources are complete; the two deferrals are preserved.
